5. cēviyar es taṉināyakam taṉatu ‘iyaṟkiyum tamiḻc ceyyuḷum” (1966) eṉṉum nūlil veḷiyiṭṭuḷḷa piṉvarum karuttu mika mukkiyamāṉatu.

“tamiḻc (caṅka) ceyyuḷ tamiḻakattilēyē karukkoṇṭu vaḷarntatu eṉpataṟkāṉa aṉittuc cāṉṟukaḷiyum koṇṭuḷḷatu. tamiḻ pēcunar veḷināṭṭiliruntu vantu iṅku kuṭiyēṟiyavarkaḷ eṉṟō, varumpoḻutē vaḷarcciyaṭinta tamiḻ moḻi, ilakkiyatti uṭaṉ koṇṭu vantaṉar eṉṟō kūṟuvataṟkuc caṅka ilakkiyam iṭam taravilli eṉpatē itaṉ poruḷ.”

6. mikap paḻiya caṅka nūlkaḷilēyē (tolkāppiyam, eṭṭuttoki nūlkaḷil aṭaṅkiya pāṭalkaḷil toṉmi cāṉṟavi) tamiḻ varivaṭivam, eḻuttumuṟi kuṟittum paṇ paṭṭa ilakkiyam kuṟittum teḷivāka, virivākak kūṟap paṭṭuḷḷatu. ai. makātēvaṉ (2003) karuttu, acōkaṉuṭiya tamiḻ - pirāmi eḻuttukkaḷiliruntu tamiḻ varivaṭivam uruvākkappaṭṭatu eṉpatākum. ikkaruttu ēṟkattakkatāka illi. kāraṇam mēṟcoṉṉa ki.mu. 300 cārnta tamiḻ nūlkaḷ, pāṭalkaḷ uruppeṟuvataṟku cila pala nūṟu āṇṭu kaḷukku muṉṉarē tamiḻ varivaṭivamum ilakkiyaṅkaḷum tōṉṟat toṭaṅkiyirukka vēṇṭum. koṟkiyil 1970il akaḻnteṭukkappaṭṭa pāṉiōṭṭil uḷḷa tamiḻ eḻuttiṉ kālam ki.mu. 700 eṉa appoḻutē uṟutiyākkap paṭṭatu. koṭumaṇalil aṇmiyil akaḻnta pāṉi ōṭṭu eḻuttukkaḷiṉ kālamum ki.mu. 500kku muntiyatāka irukka vēṇṭum eṉa irājaṉ (2004) niṟuviyuḷḷār. āticcanallūril 2004il naṭanta akaḻvāyvil kaṇṭa pāṉi ōṭṭup poṟippiṉ kālamum karimak kaṇippiṉpaṭi ki.mu. 700ai oṭṭiyatāka amiya lām eṉac ceyti vantuḷḷatu. ivaṟṟiyum piṟa ātāraṅkaḷiyum cuṭṭi irājaṉ (2004), naṭaṉakācinātaṉ (2004) ākiyōr “tamiḻukku ki.mu. 800ai oṭṭiyē oru taṉi vari vaṭivam iruntirukkavēṇṭum. ataṉip piṉpaṟṟiyē acōka tamiḻ - pirāmi varivaṭivam (lipi) ki.mu. 300il uruvākkap paṭṭirukkum” eṉpataṟkāṉa valuvāṉa kāraṇaṅkaḷi muṉ vittuḷḷaṉar. cintuveḷi nākarika lipi tirāviṭamoḻi cārntatu āṉapaṭiyāl pirāmi lipiyum atiliruntē uruvākiyirukka vēṇṭum eṉpar hīrās (New Review; cūli 1936). taṉatu paṇṭit tamiḻ eḻuttukkaḷ 1938 nūlil ti.nā. cuppiramaṇiyaṉ kūṟiyatiyum niṉivu koḷḷa vēṇṭum.

“pirāmi lipi mutalil vaṭamoḻikkāka ēṟpaṭavilliyeṉṟum uyir eḻuttukkaḷuḷ a, i, u ākiya mūṉṟukkum atikac ciṟappiyaḷippatum, meyyeḻuttukkaḷuḷ varkka eḻuttukkaḷik koḷḷātatumāṉa oru pāṣikkeṉa amik kappaṭṭup piṉṉāl vaṭamoḻikku upayōkappaṭum paṭi putiya kuṟikaḷ uṇṭākkappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟum eṇṇa vēṇṭiyirukkiṟatu. intiyāvil uḷḷa moḻikaḷuḷ ivvitam uḷḷatu tamiḻ oṉṟutāṉ. aṅṅaṉamāyiṉ pirāmi mutalil tamiḻukkeṉa aminta lipiyāka iruttal kūṭumā? iḵtu ārāy taṟkuriyatu”.

vaṭanāṭṭu varalāṟṟaṟiñar es.ār. kōyal (1979) karuttum acōka pirāmi eḻuttu paṭippaṭiyāka (vaṭanāṭṭil) uruvāṉa talla; atu oru kālakaṭṭattil (ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 300 eṉak kūṟalām) putitāka uruvākkappaṭṭatu eṉpatākum.

7. tamiḻnāṭṭil irunta ki.mu. 1900 - ki.mu. 300 cārnta peruṅkaṟkāla nākarikattiṉ toṭarcciyē kaḻaka ilakkiya kālam; annākarikak kūṟukaḷiṟ perumpālāṉavi kaḻaka ilakkiyattil kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpatum rājaṉ pōṉṟavar kaḷuṭiya aṭutta muṭivākum. āka ivvārāyccikaḷ mēlum muṉṉēṟum poḻutu tolkāppiyattiṉ kālam ki.mu. 300kkum cila nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ muṟpaṭṭatu eṉa aṉivarum ēṟṟuk koḷḷum vāyppuḷḷatu.

8. ittaruṇattil iṉṉoṉṟiyum kūṟa vēṇṭum. ki.mu. 300kku muṟpaṭṭa kaḻaka ilakkiyaṅkaḷ eviyumē namakkuk kiṭikkavilli. ki.mu. 300 - ki.pi. 300 kālakaṭṭattic cārnta caṅka ilakkiyaṅkaḷum muḻumiyāka namakkuk kiṭikkavilli. ki.pi. 300ai oṭṭik kaḻaka ilakkiyaṅkaḷit tokuttavarkaḷ evvevaṟṟi viṭṭu vikkalām eṉṟu karutiṉārkaḷō avaṟṟi maṭṭum tokuttuviṭṭu maṟṟavaṟṟi nīkkiviṭṭaṉar. tokuttavaṟṟilum māṟṟaṅkaḷ ceytirukkalām. nuṇṇaṟi vuṭiyavarkaḷ itupaṟṟi 1871-liruntu terivittuḷḷavaṟṟik kāṇpōm:-

cārlas ī kōvar : teṉṉintiya nāṭṭār pāṭal kaḷ (The Folk songs of Southern India) 1871: tiṭṭamiṭṭuc citikkappaṭāta allatu māṟṟiyeḻutappaṭāta tolpaḻaṅ kālat tamiḻnūl etiyum iṉṟu kāṇpatu aritu. tamiḻ makkaḷ - tirāviṭa (paḻantamiḻ) ilakkiyattik kiviṭṭu purāṇakkatikaḷi nampiṉāltāṉ taṅkaḷukkup piḻippu uṇṭu eṉṟu uṇarntavarkaḷē ivvāṟu paḻantamiḻ ilakkiyattic citittavarkaḷ āvar. (taṉit) tamiḻc ceyyuḷ nūlkaḷ pala matippiḻantu oḻiyumpaṭi avarkaḷ ceytaṉar. aṟavē oḻikka muṭiyāta nērvukaḷilum annūlkaḷi ayōk kiyat taṉamākac citittu urumāṟṟi ulavaviṭṭu mūlanūlkaḷiṉ karutti uṇara muṭiyātavāṟu ceytuviṭṭaṉar. civattampi (1986)yum ikkaruttiṉarē.

hayūnevil : ti tapropēṉiyaṉ (The Taprobanian) cūṉ 1896 “maturit tamiḻc caṅkamum ataṉ nūlakamum aḻikkap paṭṭapoḻutu paḻantamiḻ nūlkaḷ cakaṭṭumēṉikku aḻikkap paṭṭaṉa. (avaṟṟuḷ camaṇak koḷki vāṭi irukkalām eṉṟa karuttil). īḻattil makāvamcam iruntatupōl pāṇṭi yarkaḷ varalāṟṟik kūṟum oru nūl maturic caṅka nūlkaḷil oṉṟāka irunta aḻikkappaṭṭu ataṟkup patilāka tiruviḷi yāṭaṟ purāṇam uruvākkappaṭṭu irukka vēṇṭum. aḻikkap paṭṭa varalāṟṟu nūliṉ citivukaḷuṭaṉ, aiṅkamarkaḷ (pārp paṉiya civa matattavarkaḷ) eṇṇam, camayam ākiyavaṟṟukkup poruttamāṉa puṉi katikaḷuṭaṉ piṉṉip piṇintu appurāṇam uruvākkappaṭṭatu”.

jāṉ spiyars “ñāṉam tōṉṟiya iṭattiliruntu” vālyūs Values II - 10 cūli 1957 : “cintuveḷi nākarika muttirikaḷil nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉa (toltamiḻ) vācakaṅkaḷ uḷḷaṉa. kaṭal koṇṭa (aḻintupōṉa) paṇṭi nūlkaḷip paṟṟit tamiḻ nūlkaḷ kuṟippiṭukiṉṟaṉa. eṉiṉum puttar kālamākiya ki.mu. 600kku muntiya nūlkaḷuḷ oṉṟukūṭa kiṭik kātataṟkāṉa kāraṇatti nām uṇara muṭikiṟatu. (tol tamiḻaruṭiya) paṇṭiya āvaṇaṅkaḷi āriyarkaḷ oṉṟu viṭāmal aḻittuviṭṭaṉar eṉpatē atu”.

te.po. mīṉāṭci cuntaraṉ : tolkāppiyam - collatikāram: kallāṭar uri (1971) : “tol kāppiyatti ataṉ āciriyar enta uruvattil viṭṭuc ceṉṟārō anta vaṭivattil atu namakku vantu cēravilli” (tolkāppiyam : poruḷatikāram marapiyalil pala iṭic cerukalkaḷ uḷḷaṉa eṉpatu tolkāppiya aṟiñarkaḷ ti. cavarirāyaṉ, kā.cuppiramaṇiyapiḷḷi, cōmacuntara pārati, veḷḷi vāraṇaṉ, ilakkuvaṉār, kamil cuvelapil, irā.iḷaṅkumaraṉ, ce.vi. caṇmukam pōṉṟavar ēṟ
ṟuḷḷatākum. kuṟippāka nālvaruṇam paṟṟik kūṟum marapiyal cūttiraṅkaḷ 71-85 iṭic cerukal eṉpatu ivvaṟiñarkaḷum (ēṉ naṭuniliyāḷar aṉivarum) ēṟpatākum.

9. caṅka ilakkiyac ciṟappiṉ kāraṇamākavē tamiḻic cemmoḻiyāka ēṟṟuḷḷaṉar. mēli nāṭukaḷil paṇṭik kālattilēyē (ippōtiya airōppiya moḻikaḷil ilakkiyaṅkaḷ eviyum tōṉṟuvataṟku muṉṉarē) ciṟanta ilakkiyaṅkaḷāka viḷaṅkiya kirēkka, ilattīṉ moḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷik “celvilakkiyam” Classical Literature eṉa airōppiyar aḻittaṉar; avviru moḻikaḷiyum “cemmoḻikaḷ” Classical languages eṉṟaṉar Classical eṉpataṉ aṭippaṭip poruḷ “mutal taramāṉa / cevviya / cemmiyāṉa” eṉpatē yākum. avviru moḻikaḷiṉ (ki.mu. 500 - ki.pi. 300 cārnta) ciṟanta paltuṟi ilakkiyaṅkaḷum antanta moḻiyilēyē tāmāka uruvāṉavi; piṟamoḻit tākkattiṉāl alla. avvilakkiyaṅkaḷum avaṟṟiṉ karuttukkaḷumē piṟṟi airōppiya moḻikaḷukku (āṅkilam, pireñcu, cerumāṉiyam, spāṉiyam, uruciyaṉ mutaliyavaṟṟukku) muṉmātiriyāka amintaṉa. piṉṉar 18 ām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ iṟutip pakutiyiliruntu ulakiṉ piṟapakutikaḷil irunta ittakiya moḻikaḷiyum cemmoḻikaḷ eṉa airōppiya moḻiyiyalaṟiñarum ilakkiya aṟiñarum ēṟkalāyiṉar;  aḻikkalāyiṉar. cīṉam, camaṟkirutam, pāracīkam, arapu mutaliyavi 1800 toṭakkattil iruntē ivvāṟu karutappaṭṭu varukiṉṟaṉa. tamiḻ toṉmiyāṉa moḻi eṉa 1816-liruntutāṉ (pirāṉcis viṭ ellis) uṇarappaṭṭatu; 1856-liruntutāṉ (kālṭuvel) ēṟkappaṭṭatu. āṉāl 2000 āṇṭukaṭku muṟpaṭṭa kaḻaka (caṅka) ilakkiyaṅkaḷ, tolkāppiyam, eṭṭuttoki, pattuppāṭṭu, tirukkuṟaḷ, cilappatikāram, maṇimēkali mutaliyavi – acciṭṭu veḷivanta kālam ki.pi. 1870-1900 āṇṭukaḷēyākum. kirēkka, ilattīṉ, camaṟkiruta tol ilakkiyaṅkaḷukkuc camamāṉa – cila kūṟukaḷil viñciya - paṇṭit tamiḻ ilakkiyam uḷḷati uṇarntatum tamiḻic cemmoḻi eṉa mēṉāṭṭaṟiñar 1920kaḷiliruntē naṭimuṟiyil ottuk koṇṭuḷḷaṉar. intiya aracu 1800liruntē camaṟkirutam, pāracīkam, arapu ākiyavaṟṟic cemmoḻikaḷāka “āḷumi naṭimuṟippaṭi” (Executive action) ēṟṟukkoṇṭu avaṟṟi aracu nitiyiliruntu celavu ceytu ūkkuvittu vantatu. 2004 aktōparil intiya naṭuvaṇaracu tamiḻic “cemmoḻi”yāka ēṟpaḷittatu. 2005 cūliyiliruntu tamiḻc cemmoḻit tiṭṭattiṉ kīḻp pala naṟpaṇikaḷi miya araciṉ cemmoḻit tamiḻ niṟuvaṉam ceytu varukiṟatu.

10. cemmoḻit tamiḻ ilakkiyaṅkaḷāṉa caṅka ilakkiyaṅkaḷ paṟṟiya cila virivāṉa ceytikaḷit tamiḻar aṉivarum aṟintu koḷvatu nalam.

tolkāppiyam

11. tolkāppiyattiṉ kālam ki.mu. 5ām nūṟṟāṇṭu mutal 3ām nūṟṟāṇṭu vari eṉpatip perumpālāṉa naṭunili aṟiñar ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. camskirutattukku (pāṣikku) ilakkaṇam varinta pāṇiṉiyiṉ kālam ki.mu. 5ām nūṟ ṟāṇṭu. pāṇiṉikku muntiya camskiruta ilakkaṇaṅkaḷum uṇṭu. vaṭa intiyāviṟkuḷ nākarikamaṟṟa āriyar nuḻinta ki.mu.1500kku muṟpaṭṭa kālattil intiyāveṅkum tamiḻiya moḻikaḷ iruntaṉa. ivaṟṟukku lipiyum iruntatu. ilakkiya vaṭivaṅkaḷum iruntu avaṟṟiṉ vaṭivaṅkaḷum karuttukaḷum rik vētattilum ēṟiyuḷḷaṉa eṉpatu uṇmi. eṉavē tol kāppiyamō allatu ataṟku muntiya tamiḻ ilakkaṇaṅkaḷō (“eṉpa”. “eṉmaṉār pulavar” pōṉṟavi ivaṟṟic cuṭṭu kiṉṟaṉa) pāṇiṉikku muṟpaṭṭaviyāka iruntirukkalām eṉac cila tamiḻaṟiñar karutuvatu ātāramaṟṟakūṟṟu alla. “tol kāppiyar” eḻutiyataṉāl “tolkāppiyam” eṉap peyar peṟṟatu eṉpatu paṇṭu toṭṭu varum marapu. āyiṉum tol kāppiyam valla aṟiñarkaḷ, ilakkuvaṉār, ca.vē. cuppira maṇiyaṉ, poṟkō, culevapil pōṉṟavarkaḷ “tol +
kāppu + iyam” = tolkāppiyam, atāvatu toṉ marapik kākkum nūl eṉak kāraṇap peyar peṟṟatu eṉpatum poruttamākavē uḷḷatu. tolkāppiya eḻuttu, col atikāraṅkaḷilum poruḷ atikāra akattiṇi, puṟattiṇi iyalkaḷilum cila māṟṟaṅkaḷ / iṭic cerukalkaḷ piṟkālattil ceyyappaṭṭirukkalām eṉpatum poruḷati kārattiṉ iṟuti ēḻu iyalkaḷilum māṟṟaṅkaḷ / iṭic cerukal mikappala eṉpatum cuvelapil, mār pōṉṟōr karuttu. pirāmaṇiya ātikkam mikunta kālattil tol kāppiyam muḻumiyāka aḻittoḻikkappaṭāmal pātukāp pataṟkeṉṟē tamiḻ, tamiḻar pāl paṟṟuḷḷavarkaḷum cila iṭiccerukalkaḷi virakākac ceytirukkalām eṉpar pāvāṇar. (eṉiṉum tolkāppiya mutalurikārar iḷam pūraṇar kālamākiya ki.pi. 11m nūṟṟāṇṭukku muṉṉarc ceytu muṭikkappaṭṭirukka vēṇṭum) tolkāppiyam “māntaṉ aṟivāṟṟal evvaḷavu viyattaku ucca nili eytakkūṭum eṉpatik kāṭṭum ciṟanta cāṉṟukaḷuḷ oṉṟu” One of the finest monuments of human intelligence eṉpar cuvelapil. ilakkiyak koḷkiyil vēṟu entap paṇṭiya moḻi
kaḷilum illāta ciṟanta karuttukkaḷit tol poruḷ. ceyyuḷiyal kūṟuvati cuvelapil (1991) kaṭṭuri viḷakku kiṟatu. tolkāppiya eḻuttatikārattil kaṭi āṟu iyal kaḷum puṇarcci paṟṟiyaṉa. “tamiḻil puṇarcci paṟṟiya ilakkaṇam taṉittaṉmikoṇṭatu. oṭṭunili viṭṭut taḷarātu, iṉ ṟaḷavum “kaṉṉit tamiḻāka” viḷaṅkuvatu, ippuṇarcci paṟṟiya aṟiviyal aṇuku muṟikaḷiṉālēyām” (tamiḻaṇṇal, 2004)

12. tolkāppiya ceyyuḷiyal kōṭpāṭṭuc ciṟappu paṟṟi cuvelepil (1974) kūṟiyuḷḷatu varumāṟu:

		tolkāppiyam aṭippaṭi nūlāka iruppatu ilakkaṇa ilakkiya āyvukaḷukku maṭṭumaṉṟu. mānta iṉap paraval cārnta puviyiyal, kumutāya māntaviyal, paṇpāṭu, cūḻniliyiyal, uḷaviyal ākiya pulaṅkaḷ cārnta ariya ceytikaḷ annūlil uḷḷaṉa; eṉavē tamiḻar nākarikam- paṇpāṭu cārnta (ēṉ potuvāṉa mānta iṉa nākarikam- paṇpāṭu cārnta) āyvukaḷukkut tolkāppiyam mika mukkiyamāṉatu. eṉiṉum ilakkiyam kūṟuvatellām  (akkālattil nilaviya) uṇmic camutāya niliyi nēraṭiyākak kāṭṭukiṟatu eṉṟu eṇṇutal apattam; uṇmi niliyi viruppuveṟuppiṉṟi aṟiviyal pūrvamākak kāṭṭukiṟatu eṉṟu eṇṇutal mēlum apattamākum. akkāla āṇ peṇmakkaḷiṉ akam, puṟa vāḻkkiyi uḷḷatu uḷḷapaṭi tatrupamāka caṅkap pulavarkaḷuṭiya pāṭalkaḷ kāṭṭuvatāka camūka varalāṟṟāciriyar cilar muṉṉark koṇṭuḷḷaṉar. āṉāl uṇmi eṉṉa? ceyyuḷ ‘uṇmi vāḻkki” nikaḻccikaḷi antanta nikaḻcci eppaṭi irukka vēṇṭumeṉṟa ilakkiyamarapu uḷḷatō anta marapukkuk kaṭṭuppaṭṭē cittarikkiṟatu. atāvatu marapukkēṟṟa māṟṟaṅ kaḷuṭaṉ tāṉ cittarikkiṟatu. “uṇmi vāḻkki” vēṟu; ati ilakkiyattil kāṭṭum marapu vēṟu; eṉpatip pulavarkaḷum poruḷilakkaṇa vāciriyarum uṇarntiruntaṉar. uṇarntiruntataṉāltāṉ uṇmi vāḻkkiyiṉ kūṟṟukaḷ ceykikaḷi ‘ulakiyal vaḻakku” eṉṟum ilakkiyattil (ceyyuḷil) vāḻkkiyic cittarikkum marapic ceyyuḷ vaḻakku “pulaṉeṟi vaḻakku” eṉṟum kuṟittaṉar. cila nikaḻccikaḷ, ceytikaḷi ippaṭi ippaṭittāṉ kāṭṭa vēṇṭum eṉṟu ilakkiyappaṭippāḷarkaḷum vācakarkaḷum tamakkuḷ icintu uruvākkum mēlvaric caṭṭamākiya marapē ilakkiya marapākum. (e.kā. ovvoru tiṇikkum uriya mutal, karu, uripporuḷkaḷ). nikaḻcci/ ceyti vācakarukkut terintatu tāṉ; ataṉi oru putiya pārviyil, kōṇattil (marapi aṭippaṭiyil mīṟāmal) kāṭṭum poḻutu vācakar anta nayatti naṉku uṇarntu pārāṭṭa iyalkiṟatu. [itaṉi pi.marutanāyakam tamatu Poetics of the lyric in classical languages” (Chemmozhi I - 2 Jan - March  2007) kaṭṭuriyil mēlum teḷivākkukiṟār] akappāṭal etuvum orutaṭavi kūṭa talivaṉ, talivi peyaric cuṭṭuvatilli. talivaṉum taliviyum eppoḻutum iṉṉār eṉṟu kuṟippāka aṭiyāḷam kāṭṭa muṭiyātavarkaḷē. eṉavē akap pāṭalkaḷ cittarikkum uṇarvukaḷ (ṭapiḷpu. ec. haṭcaṉ taṉatu “ilakkiyap payiṟcikkāṉa muṉṉuri” 1946 nūlpakkam 97l colvatu pōla “potuvāka enta āṭavaṉukkum peṇṇukkum poruntuvatāka irukkumēyaṉṟi ivaṉukkut tāṉ/ivaḷukkuttāṉ poruntum eṉṟu irukkātu. (uḷḷa uṇarvukaḷi vivarikkātu”) veḷiyulaki vivarikkum puṟappāṭalkaḷil cila potuvāka aṉivarum nōkkum pārviyil irukkum; cila antantap pulavaruṭiya taṉi nōkkil amintatāka irukkum. piṉṉavaṟṟukku eṭuttuk kāṭṭu kiyaṟu nilippāṭalkaḷ. vaḷḷalkaḷ, naṇparkaḷ iṟantāl pulavarkaḷ tam neñciṉ āḻattiliruntu uṇarvu kaḷik koṭṭip pāṭum tuyarap pāṭalkaḷ ovvoṉṟum taṉittaṉmi vāyntavi; cātāraṇa kirāmiya oppārikaḷiṉ vālāyap paṭimaṅkaḷik koṇṭaviyalla”.

13. tamiḻccemmoḻi ilakkiyattiṉ mukāmi nūl kaḷākiya eṭṭuttoki, pattuppāṭṭu ākiyavaṟṟiṉ (vēṟu entac cemmoḻi ilakkiyattukkum illāta) ciṟappukaḷāka kamil cuvelapil, jārj lūcarṉ hārṭ, ēkē. rāmāṉujaṉ pi. maruta nāyakam ākiyōr terivittuḷḷa karuttukkaḷ cila varumāṟu. (innālvarumē caṅka ilakkiyatti āḻntu payiṉṟavarkaḷ; piṟacemmoḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷip paṟṟiyum terintavarkaḷ; nuṇmāṇuḻipula mikkavarkaḷ):

		“eṭṭuttoki, pattuppāṭṭu nūlkaḷ (mottam 2300 pāṭalkaḷ) acciṭṭu ulakiṉ pārvikku mutalil vantatu 1880 - 1910 kāla aḷavil ākum. avi veḷivanta akkaṇamē (atuvari cāmāṉiyap piṟkāla intiya moḻikaḷil oṉṟākak karutappaṭṭatu māṟi ulakiṉ ciṟantu cemmoḻikaḷil oṉṟākat tamiḻ ākiviṭṭatu. pireñcu āciyaviyalaṟiñar piyar meyl “kirēkka uṇarccik kavitikaḷil tali ciṟanta
navamaṇikaḷukku iṇiyāṉavi iccaṅka ilakkiyap pāṭal kaḷ; intiyāvil, ēṉ ulakil, ilakkiyap paṭippukaḷiṉ cikaraṅkaḷil oṉṟu ippāṭalkaḷ” eṉṟu ceytuḷḷa
matippīṭṭi caṅkilakkiyap pāṭalkaḷi āḻntu payiṉṟuḷḷa evarum ēṟṟuk koḷvar.”
- cuvelapil (1960)

		“ulakiṉ nākarikam - paṇpāṭṭukku tirāviṭarkaḷ, kuṟip pākat tamiḻarkaḷ ceytuḷḷa ciṟanta paṅkaḷippukaḷ pallava, cōḻar kāla kōyiṟ kaṭṭaṭakkali; cōḻar kāla ceppup paṭimaṅkaḷ; parata nāṭṭiyam eṉa aḻikkappaṭum tamiḻar naṭaṉak kali; karunāṭaka ici eṉa aḻikkappaṭum tamiḻ ici mutaliya palavām; avaṟṟuḷḷum talimi cāṉṟa ciṟappu vāyntatu (caṅka kālac cemmoḻit tamiḻ) ilakkiyam. piṟamoḻi, piṟa nāṭṭu aṟiñar iti uṇarntu ivvilakki yattip payiṉṟu ulaka mānta iṉattiṉ iṉṟiyamiyāta uṉṉatamāṉa marapuc celvamāka makiḻntu pōṟṟa vēṇṭum. uccanilikku muḻut takutiyuṭiya avvilakkiyattiṟku itu vari annili tarappaṭavilli.”
- cuvelapil (1973)

“intiyāviṉ piṟa enta moḻi ilakkiyattilum illāta ciṟappuk kūṟukaḷik koṇṭatu eṉpatu maṭṭu maṉṟip paṇṭit tamiḻ (caṅka) ilakkiyam uṉṉatamāṉa ilakkiya nayamum vāyntatu. ulakiṉ ciṟanta cemmoḻikaḷ variciyil tamiḻukkuriya iṭatti caṅka ilakkiyattiṉ 26350 varikaḷ koṇṭa pāṭalkaḷ peṟṟut tantuviṭṭaṉa. av vilakkiyattiṉ ciṟappi ippoḻutu tāṉ ulaku uṇarat toṭaṅkiyuḷḷatu.”

“aci, cīr, taḷi aṭippaṭiyilāṉa yāppiyal, akam puṟak kōṭpāṭu; uvamaviyal, ākiya aṉittilum taṉit taṉmi vāynta ciṟanta ilakkiyak koḷki koṇṭa caṅka nūlkaḷi uruvākkiya perumi tamiḻp paṇpāṭṭukku maṭṭumē uriya ciṟappākum.

“intiyāvil tōṉṟi vaḷarnta cevviyal ilakkiyam - cevviyal moḻip perum paṇpāṭukaḷ iraṇṭu – oṉṟu camaṟkirutam; maṟṟatu tamiḻ; avi oṉṟi oṉṟu cārntavi yalla - cutantaramāṉavi.

“intiya moḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷilēyē tamiḻilakkiyam tāṉ cevvilakkiyamākavum, nikaḻkāla iyakkamākavum orē nērattil ilaṅkum ciṟappu vāyntatu; atu camaṟkirutac cevviyal ilakkiyam aḷavu toṉmi vāyntatu; paṇṭi kirēkka moḻik kavitikaḷi eppaṭic cevvilakkiyam eṉa ulaku karutukiṟatō atē taṉmi vāyntatu tamiḻc caṅka ilakkiyam. uyirt tuṭippuḷḷa ilakkiyaṅkaḷ iṉṟum uruvākum moḻiyākavum tamiḻ uḷḷatu.

“tamiḻc cevvilakkiyam (caṅka ilakkiyam) makkaḷip paṟṟiya ilakkiyam; makkaḷ uruvākkiyatu - āṉāl “nāṭṭup puṟa” ilakkiyamaṉṟu”
- cuvelapil (1973)

“vāḻkki iṉiyatu; makiḻcci taruvatu. cāvu el lōrukkum varuvatu. ivvulaka vāḻviliruntu viṭutali peṟa vēṇṭum eṉṟa muyaṟciyellām illi. vaḷḷaṇmi
yum nayaṉmiyum iyalpāka amikiṉṟaṉa - āṉmīka nalaṉuk kākavō parikāramākavō alla. iṟantapiṉṉar vīrarum cāṉṟōrum tuṟakkattil niliyāka vāḻvar; tīyōr mīḷānirayam eytuvar eṉak karutiṉar. nila ulakil pukaḻi niṟīic ceṉṟavar tuṟakkattil iṉiyavāḻvu eṉṉum vekumati peṟuvar: kātal, puṇarcci, tirumaṇam, makkaṭpēṟu ivi mānta vāḻvil niṟivu peṟuvataṟkāṉa vaḻimuṟikaḷ eṉak karutappaṭṭaṉa. vaḷḷaṇmi, vīracceyal, piṟarkkuriyāḷaṉāka vāḻtal - ivaṟṟil oruvaṉ celaviṭṭa nāṭkaḷi niṉittup pulavarum piṟarum kūṟum pukaḻ moḻikaḷ maṭṭumē oruvaṉ cāvukkup piṉṉar niliniṟpaviyākum.”
- cuvelapil (1974)

“ekkālattukkum poruntum aḻakiyal kūṟukaḷ, viḻumaṅkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟi caṅka ilakkiyaṅkaḷ nirampak koṇ ṭavi. iraṇṭāyiram āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar yātta avvilakkiyaṅkaḷ piṟkālattil (atāvatu navīṉakālat tiṟaṉāyvāḷar) vakuttuḷḷa ilakkiya nayakkōṭpāṭukaḷiṉ paṭiyum uṉṉatamāka ēṟkappaṭuvaṉavāka uḷḷaṉa.

“caṅka ilakkiyattip payilum oru camutāyamē (taṉinapar maṭṭumalla) avvilakkiya viḻumaṅkaḷi eyta iyalum vakiyil, eyta viḻiyum vakiyil avvilakkiyam amintuḷḷatu. eṉavē tāṉ taṟkāla ilakkiyat tiṟaṉāyvāḷarum pōṟṟum vakiyil ulaka māntar aṉivarum matikkat takka “eṉṟu muḷa” cirañcīvittaṉmi koṇṭuḷḷatu caṅka ilakkiyam”
- cuvelapil (1974)

“paḻantamiḻ ilakkiyamum paṇpāṭum paṭitta akam - puṟakkōṭpāṭukaḷ ulakilēyē taṉittaṉmiyum ciṟappum vāyntavi. cila kūṟukaḷil tamiḻilakkiyattukkum camaṟkiruta ilakkiyattukkum oppumi kāṇappaṭalām. paṭṭāl eṉṉa? camaṟkiruta ilakkiyattiliruntu tāṉ tamiḻ ilakkiyam kaṭaṉ peṟṟirukkum eṉa ēṉ karuta vēṇṭum? (nērmāṟākavum nikaḻntirukkalām aṉṟō?)”
- cuvelapil (1975)

“potuvāṉa ceytiyi, teḷivāka, muḻumiyāṉa nuṇṇiya pārviyil nōkki, eḷiya āṉāl viram pōṉṟa coṟkaḷil, āḻnta poruḷuṭaṉ taruvatē ciṟanta kaviti eṉat taṟkālattil karutappaṭukiṟatu. veḷippaṭiyāṉa orē ceytiyit taruvativiṭa aṭukkaṭukkāṉa pala poruḷit taravallatāṉa coṟceṭṭumikka pāṭalē ciṟanta kavitit taṉmiyuṭiyatākak karutappaṭukiṟatu. tolkāppiyam nōkku, payaṉ, eccam (colleccam, kuṟippeccam) uḷḷuṟi, iṟicci eṉṟu kūṟuvatellām itu tāṉ;
- pi. marutanāyakam (2007)

“mutalāvatāka, tamiḻiṉ toṉmi kuṟippiṭattakkatu. taṟkālattiya piṟa intiya moḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷukku ōrāyiram āṇṭu muṉṉar uruvāṉa ilakkiyattik koṇṭatu tamiḻ. tamiḻiṉāti nūl ākiya tolkāppiyattiṉ pakutikaḷ ki.mu. 200ai oṭṭiyavi eṉpatu mikap paḻiya [tamiḻik] kal veṭṭukaḷiliruntu terikiṟatu. paḻantamiḻp perunūlkaḷāṉa eṭṭuttoki, pattuppāṭṭu mutaliyavi ki.pi oṉṟu - iraṇṭu nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷic cārntavi. intiyāviṉ miku toṉmi vāynta, camayam cārāta (secular) pāṭalkaḷ aviyē; kāḷitācaṉ kāviyaṅkaḷukku irunūṟu āṇṭu muṟpaṭṭavi.”

“iraṇṭāvatāka, tamiḻ ilakkiyamarapu maṭṭum tāṉ (camskirutattiliruntu peṟappaṭāta) intiya maṇṇukkuriya ilakkiya marapu ākum. teṉṉāṭṭil camaṟkiruta tākkam valuppaṭuvataṟku muṉṉarē mukiḻttatu atu. camaṟkiruta/piṟa intiya moḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷiliruntu māṟupaṭṭa taṉmi yuṭiyatu atu. tamiḻ taṉakkeṉṟu taṉi ilakkiyak koḷki, ilakkaṇamarapu, murukiyal (aesthetics) uṭiyatu; ivaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭiyil aminta tamiḻc cemmoḻi ilakkiyam vēṟu emmoḻi ilakkiyattukkum illāta taṉittaṉmi vāyntatu (unique). camskirutattilō vēṟu intiya moḻikaḷilō kāṇaviyalāta nayamāṉa uṇarvi (sensibility – intiya maṇṇukkē uriya uṇarvip paḻantamiḻ ilakkiyattil kāṇalām; tamiḻppulavarkaḷ paṉṉeṭuṅkālam vaḷart teṭutta ceḻumiyāṉa uṇarvup pārampariyam atu.

“mūṉṟāvatāka, camskirutam - ilattīṉ, cīṉam, pāracīkam, arapu ākiya moḻikaḷiṉ ilakkiyaṅkaḷil ciṟantavaṟṟukku iṇiyāṉa taram vāyntatu tamiḻc cemmoḻi ilakkiyam. avvilakkiyam nuṇmāṇuḻipulamikkatu; palvēṟu pāṭuporuḷ koṇṭatu (muṟkāla intiya ilakkiyaṅkaḷil cāmāṉiyarkaḷip paṟṟiyum niṟiyap pāṭiyatu tamiḻilakkiyam maṭṭumē); mānta iṉattukkup potumiyāṉa viḻumiyaṅ kaḷik kūṟiyatu. eṉavē piṟa cevviyal moḻikaḷiṉ marapu kaḷuṭaṉum ilakkiyaṅkaḷuṭaṉum camamāka niṟkum uraṉ vāyn tatu atu. ulakiṉ taliciṟanta aṟanūlkaḷil oṉṟu tirukkuṟaḷ. tamiḻccevviyal ilakkiyattil atu pōṉṟu pala tuṟikaḷilum ciṟanta nūlkaḷ uṇṭu. avvilakkiyam pāṭit tulakkam tarāta mānta iṉa aṉupavam oṉṟum illi.

“iṟutiyākat taṟkāla intiyap paṇpāṭṭiyum marapiyum uṇarvataṟkup payilavēṇṭiya ilakkiyaṅkaḷil tamiḻilakkiyam mutaṉmi vāyntatu. camskirutak kāviyaṅ kaḷil teṉṉaka maraput tākkam uḷḷatu kuṟittu nāṉ virivāka eḻutiyuḷḷēṉ. caṅkakālam toṭaṅki tamiḻilil pāṭappaṭṭa ciṟanta intu camaya pakti ilakkiya nūlkaḷ tām taṟpōtiya intu camayattukku aṭittaḷamāka irukkiṉṟaṉa. tamiḻ pakti ilakkiyak karuttukaḷ pākavata purāṇattilum piṟa (teluṅku/ kaṉṉaṭa/camskiruta) pakti nūlkaḷilum ēṟṟappaṭṭu intiyā muḻuvatum paraviṉa. vētattukkuc camamākak karutappaṭum tamiḻ [tivyapirapanta] pakti ilakkiyap pāṭalkaḷ teṉṉintiyāvil periya viṇavakkōvilkaḷil (tiruppati pōṉṟavi) vētattōṭu cērttu ōtappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. taṟkāla intō āriya moḻikaḷukku camskirutamē mūlamoḻi; atupōla taṟkālat tamiḻukkum maliyāḷattukkum cevviyal tamiḻē mūla moḻiyākum.

		“ulakattiṉ ciṟanta cemmoḻikaḷil tamiḻum oṉṟu eṉpatu ittuṟiyil valla aṟiñarukku uḷḷaṅki nellik kaṉi pōlat terinta uṇmiyākum”
- jārj el. hārṭ (2003)

“iraṇṭāyiram āṇṭukaḷukkum atikamāṉa varalāṟu uṭiya mikaccila ulaka moḻikaḷil tamiḻ oṉṟu. ulakiṉ pāriya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ oṉṟil atutāṉ, miya iṭam vakikkiṉṟatu. tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam cārnta emmoḻi āyvāḷarukkum toṭakkattiliruntē tamiḻ, tamiḻ varalāṟu tamiḻiṉ paṇṭi ilakkaṇam ākiyavaṟit teriya vēṇṭiyatu iṉṟiyamiyātatu. tamiḻ ilakkiyac celvatti viñcum ilakkiyam ulakil vēṟu emmoḻiyilum illi. eṉavē tamiḻ ilakkiyac ciṟappi ulakeṅkum aṉivarum terintu koḷḷa vaki ceyyum moḻipeyarppup paṇikaḷ tēvi.”
- ār.ī. āṣar

caṅka kāla akapporuḷ nūlkaḷ

14. caṅka ilakkiya akapporuḷ pāṭalkaḷiṉ taṉic ciṟappi ē.kē. rāmāṉucaṉ (1969) pārāṭṭiyuḷḷatu varumāṟu:-

“toṉmiyāṉaviyē eṉiṉum iṉṟum uyirūṭṭam uḷḷaviyāy ilaṅkum inta (caṅka ilakkiya) akap pāṭal kaḷip pōṉṟa nayam vāyntavi intiya ilakkiyaṅkaḷ vēṟu evaṟṟilum illi. appāṭalkaḷ kūṟum vāḻkki neṟiyilum, vivarikkum nikaḻccikaḷilum ciṟanta cemmoḻi ilak kiyattiṉ piṉvarum kūṟukaḷ uḷḷaṉa: kātalōṭu kaṉivum paṇpāṭum; veḷippaṭik kūṟṟukkaḷōṭu uḷḷuṟi, iṟicci, aṅkatam ākiyaviyum; talivaṉ, talivi peyar cuṭṭappaṭāviṭiṉum ōviyam pōṉṟa nuṇṇiya varuṇaṉi; aṭikaḷ cila; avi cuṭṭum poruḷō peritu. tamiḻar aṟivuttiṟaṉiṉ mikat toṉmiyāṉa eṭuttukkāṭṭu iv vakappāṭalkaḷ; atu maṭṭumalla kaṭanta iraṇṭāyiram āṇṭu tamiḻ ilakkiya paṭippil ivaṟṟi viñcuvaṉa illi.”

15. akap pāṭalkaḷiṉ amippu paṟṟi cuvelapil (1986) viḷakkamum karuttiṟkoḷḷat takkatu.
	
		“akappāṭal ovvoṉṟum oru kūṟṟu: talivaṉ, talivi, tōḻi, cevili mutaliya cilaruḷ oruvar, nāṭaka māntar pōl, kūṟuvatāka atu amiyum. avarkaḷ oruvaruk koruvarō, taṉakkut tāṉākavō, nilavi nōkkiyō kūṟuvati vācakarkaḷākiya nām kēṭkiṟōm; avvaḷavē. nāṭakak kāṭci oṉṟi muḻumiyāka akappāṭal oṉṟil uḷḷa kūṟṟu vivarittuviṭum. ivi nāṭṭuppāṭalkaḷ pōl virintu pāṭiyavi alla; oru nikaḻcciyik kaṇṭa allatu eṇṇippārtta oru kaviñar atu paṟṟiya tamatu eṇṇaṅkaḷi nērākavō urumāṟṟiyō veḷippaṭuttiya pāṭalkaḷum alla. ivi aracavippāṭalkaḷiṉ naḷiṉam uṭiyavi; “vikalum eṇtēr ceyyum taccaṉ tiṅkaḷ valitta kāl pōl” nuṇṇiya pulamittiṟaṉuṭaṉ yākkap paṭṭavi.

		16. mēṟkaṇṭa iruvar kūṟṟukkaḷiṉ āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷ varumāṟu:

		“In their antiquity and in their contemporaniety, there is not much else in any Indian literature equal to these quiet and dramatic Tamil poems. In their values and stances they represent a mature classical poetry : passion is balanced by courtesy, transparency by ironies and nuances of design, impersonality by vivid detil, leanness of line by richness of implication. These poems are not just the earliest evidence of the Tamil genius. The Tamils in all their 2000 years of literary effort wrote nothing better”.
- A.K. Ramanujan.

		An Aham poem is always a “Kurru”, an utterance by one of the limited number of dramatis persone, (hero, heroine, heroine’s girl-friend, foster mother ete). The reader only overhears what the characters say to each other, to themselves, or to the moon. A poem in this tradition implies, evokes, enacts a drama in a monologue. The poems are not the result of rapid composition like oral epics or as lyrical expessions or sublimations of the poet’s own feelings triggered by some romantic event. They are the result of skillful and subtle care, the sophistication of court poetry, “like a chariot wheel made thoughtfully over a month by a carpenter who tosses off eight chariots in a day.” 
- K.V. Zvelebil. 



puṟappāṭalkaḷ : puṟanāṉūṟu

17. puṟanāṉūṟṟil iṉṟu kiṭṭiyuḷḷa 368 pāṭalkaḷiṉ vakippāṭu varumāṟu : cērar-22; cōḻar-61; pāṇṭiyar-31; kuṟunila maṉṉar -134; piṟa-120. hārṭ (1999) “āriyarukku muṟpaṭṭa teṉṉintiyāvi, ēṉ perumaḷavukku āriyarukku muntiya intiyāviyē vivarikkum āvaṇam puṟanāṉūṟu eṉpar” - a testament of pre-Aryan South India and, to a significant extent, of pre Aryan India..” avarum hāṅk hipeṭs-m cērntu 1999-l puṟanāṉūṟu muḻuvatiyum āṅkilattil pāṭalkaḷāka kavinayattuṭaṉ moḻipeyarttuḷḷaṉar:- The four hundred songs of war and wisdom” (Columbia university Press; New york). 

patiṟṟuppattu

18. patiṟṟuppattil mutal pattum iṟutippattum tavira eṭṭuppattukaḷi cārnta 80 pāṭalkaḷē iṉṟu kiṭṭi yuḷḷaṉa. ovvoru pattukkum oru patikam uḷḷatu. avi piṉṉar eḻutic cērkkappaṭṭavi. pāṭalkaḷil kūṟappaṭāta ceytikaḷiyum toṉmak katikaḷiyum patikaṅkaḷ kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. varalāṟṟuc ceytikaḷ pala iruppiṉum cēra maṉṉarip pukaḻvataṟkeṉṟē eḻutiya ippāṭalkaḷ puṟanāṉūṟṟil uḷḷa ciṟanta kavinayam vāynta pāṭalkaḷiṉ taram uṭiyaṉavāka illi. patiṟṟuppattu muḻuvatiyum āṅkilattil uri naṭiyil peyarttuḷḷavar ē.vi.cuppiramaṇiyaṉ (1980).

tirukkuṟaḷ

19. “aṟitōraṟiyāmi kaṇṭaṟṟāl” eṉpatu tiruk kuṟaḷukkum poruntuvatākum. kuṟaḷiṉ ciṟappukkaḷip pārāṭṭip palamoḻi, pala nāṭṭu aṟiñarkaḷ kūṟiyavaṟṟit tokuttāl atuvē oru periya nūlākiviṭum! ulakap periyārkaḷil oruvarāṉa ālperṭ cuviṭcar 1936il veḷiyiṭṭa “intiyac cintaṉiyum ataṉ vaḷarcciyum” (Indian Thought and its Development; Hodder and Stoughton; London; PP. 272) eṉṉum nūliṉ 200-205 pakkaṅkaḷil intiyat tattuvañāṉikaḷil tiruvaḷḷuvar taliciṟantu viḷaṅkuvatip pārāṭṭiyuḷḷār. cila pakutikaḷ varumāṟu:

“intiya makkaḷiṭiyē paṇṭik kālattiliruntē piṟa uyirkaḷ aṉittukkum aṉpukāṭṭi utavum aṟakkoḷki ((the idea of active love) iruntu vantati, intiyap paṇṭi ilakkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇum katikaḷil iruntu aṟikiṟōm. attuṭaṉ kuṟippākat tirukkuṟaḷ eṉṉum nūlil kāṇum aṟavuric ceyyuḷkaḷiliruntum aṟikiṟōm”.

“(ulakil piṟa uyirkaḷiṭattu aṉpu kāṭṭic ceyal paṭuvati (life Affirmation) avvaḷavāka valiyuṟuttāta maṉu tarma cāttiram pōṉṟavaṟṟiliruntu māṟupaṭum) kuṟaḷil ulakiyum vāḻkkiyiyum veṟuttu otukkum (world and life negation) paravalākak kāṇappaṭuvatu alla. eṅkō ōriru iṭattil vēṇṭumāṉāl irukkalām.

“piramaṇīyam, puttamatam, pakavatkīti pōṉṟavaṟṟip pōl immi maṟumip payaṉ karutiyē aṟaneṟiyil niṟṟal vēṇṭum eṉṟa vāṇikak koḷkiyik kuṟaḷ valiyuṟuttu vatu illi. nallatu ceyvatē takkatu eṉṟa uṇarviṉā lēyē nallatu (aṟam) ceyya vēṇṭum eṉṟu kuṟaḷ kūṟukiṟatu (kuṟaḷkaḷ 222, 211). pakavatkītiyō karumam ceytu koṇ ṭiruppatu pirapañcaniyati eṉṟu vaṟaṭṭuttaṉamāka valintu kūṟukiṟatu. āṉāl, eṉṉa viyappu, kuṟaḷō maṉitaṉ uḻip patum īṭṭuvatum piṟarukkum piṟa uyirkaḷukkum avaṉ naṉmi ceyvataṟkākattāṉ eṉṟu cāṟṟukiṟatu. (kuṟaḷkaḷ 81, 212.) pakavat kītippaṭi kaṭami jātikkut takkapaṭi vēṟupaṭum kuṟaḷō makkaḷ aṉivarum ‘nallavi ceytoḻukak” kūṟukiṟatu.”

“ēṉi intiya tarmacāstirakkārar uḻippatil, muyaṟci ceyvatil makiḻvōṭu īṭupaṭu eṉṟu kūṟavilli. kuṟaḷkaḷ 619, 630, pōṉṟaviyē ulakiyalilum, vāḻkkiyilum, ārvat tuṭaṉ īṭupaṭumpaṭi aṟivuṟuttukiṉṟaṉa.

“(puttamum kītiyum pōlap paṟṟiṉmi, veṟup piṉmi, kollāmi, akimci ākiyavaṟṟiyum kuṟaḷ valiyuṟuttiṉālum kūṭa atē camayattil) kuṟaḷ atikamāka valiyuṟuttuvatu aṉpum aruḷumē yām (the living ethic of love). kāṇka kuṟaḷkaḷ 72, 78, 79, 103, 226, 241.

“aṟaneṟicār ulaka vāḻkki ilaṭciyattit tiṟam paṭac cuṭṭukiṟatu kuṟaḷ. maṉitaṉiṉ taṉi vāḻkki, piṟa uyir kaḷōṭum ulakattōṭum uṟaḻum avaṉ vāḻkki ākiya vaṟṟil kikkoḷḷa vēṇṭiya neṟikaḷik kuṟaḷ viyattaku paṇpuṭaṉum cālpuṭaṉum naṭimuṟikkukanta vakiyil vakut tuḷḷatu. (Characterised by nobility and good sense). ulaka ilakkiyattil vēṟu eṅkum ivvaḷavu ciṟanta oḷimayamāṉa aṟavuri vācakaṅkaḷi vaḻaṅkum nūlik kāṇa iyalātu.: (kuṟaḷkaḷ 92, 105, 108, 121, 159, 162, 216, 298, 319, 578, 594, 628, 757, 782, 874, 931, 973, 999, 1007, 1024, 1032)”.

“āka kiṟittuva ūḻit toṭakkak kālattilēyē intiya makkaḷiṭiyē ulakavāḻkkiyiṭamum uyirkaḷiṭamum paṇpum aṉpum kāṭṭum oru taṉmi iyalpāka iruntu vantuḷḷatik kuṟaḷiliruntu aṟikiṟōm. pirāmaṇiyam, puttam, pakavatkīti cār intumatam ākiyavaṟṟiṉ kōṭ pāṭukaḷil illātatu atu. āṉāl kīḻcātiyiṉariṭamum, cātāraṇa makkaḷiṭamum tōṉṟi makkaḷōṭu makkaḷāka vāḻnta ciṟanta camayap pōtakarkaḷ mūlamāka attaṉmi metumetuvāka nāḷaṭivil intu matattil iṭam peṟa lāyiṟṟu”.

20. kirāliṉ tirukkuṟaḷ cerumaṉ moḻipeyarppip paṭittuviṭṭuttāṉ ālparṭ cuviṭcar 1936 mēṟkaṇṭa vāṟu viyantu pārāṭṭiṉār “Free Hindusthan”  eṉṟa peyaril tārakanāt tās (1884-1958) amerikkāvil naṭattiya itaḻukku tālstāyiṭam oru kaṭṭuri kēṭṭār. tācukku 14-12-1908 aṉṟu tālstāy oru kaṭṭuri aṉuppiṉār; “māntar vāḻkkikkut tēviyāṉa orē viti aṉpuṭimi; aṉ puṭimikkuk kaṭṭaḷikkal tīyavi ceytārkkum iṉṉā ceyyāmiyēyākum” [Great religious teachers have lid down] one invariable condition of love, namely the enduring of injuries, insults and violence of all kinds without resisting evil by evil. eṉṟu akkaṭṭuriyil eḻutiyiruntār mēlum taṉ karuttukkāṉa pala ātāraṅkaḷil oṉṟākat tirukkuṟaḷililiruntu

“iṉṉā ceytārkkum...”
“aḵki akaṉṟa......
“piṟarkku iṉṉā......

mutaliya nālintu kuṟaḷkaḷiṉ āṅkila moḻi peyarppiyum tantiruntār. mēlum atē kaṭṭuriyil tālstāy “maṟu piṟavi” uṭpaṭa pala mūṭa nampikkikaḷi viṭṭuviṭa vēṇṭum eṉṟum eḻutiṉār [“If only people freed themselves from their beliefs in all kinds of Ormuzds, Bhramas and their incarnation as Krishnas and Christs, from belief in paradise and hell, in reincarnations and resurrections, interference of gods in external affairs and infallibility of Veda, Bible, Koran etc”] ‘pirī intustāṉ’” itaḻil vanta ikkaṭṭuriyip paṭitta makātmā kānti teṉ āppirikkāviliruntu tālstāykku  1- 10 1909 aṉṟu kaṭitam eḻutiṉār tālstāyiṉ 14 - 12 - 1908 kaṭitatti acciṭṭup parappa aṉumati kēṭṭu. [intiyar palarum maṟupiṟaviyi (reincarnation) nampuvatāl mēṟkaṇṭa vācakattil reincarnation ai nīkkiviṭa tālstāyiṭam aṉumati kēṭṭār kānti; “nīkkavēṇṭām, eṉpatu eṉkaruttu eṉiṉum tāṅkaḷ virumpiṉāl nīkkikkoḷka” eṉṟu tālstāy maṟu moḻiviṭuttār 7-10-1909 aṉṟu] aṉumati vantavuṭaṉ tālstāyiṉ 14-12-1908 kaṭitatti kāntiyaṭikaḷ acciṭṭu āyirakkaṇakkil parappiṉār. icceytiyiṉ virivip piṉvarum nūlilkāṇalām: 

		Christian Bartolf (Ed) 1997: “Letter to a Hindoo: Tarakanath Das, LeoTolstoy and Mahatma Gandhi” Gandhi – Information Zentrum; Berlin; Selbs tverlag; pp. 80 

21. tirukkuṟaḷ ivvaḷavu ciṟappu vāyntatāka iruppiṉum 19-20ām nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil ēṟpaṭṭa tamiḻ, tamiḻar maṟumalarccikku muṉṉar entat tamiḻ maṉṉaṉum aracum kuṟaḷip piṉpaṟṟiyatāka entak kalveṭṭum, ceppēṭum kuṟippiṭṭatilli’ māṟāka vēta neṟi vaḻuvātum “oru kulattukkoru nīti” kūṟum maṉusmruti pōṉṟavaṟṟip piṉpaṟṟiyum āṇṭatākavē kalveṭṭukaḷ pōṉṟavaṟṟil tamiḻ maṉṉarkaḷ pīṟṟik koṇṭaṉar!

cilappatikāramum maṇimēkaliyum

22. cilappatikāramum maṇimēkaliyum cātāraṇa makkaḷik kāviyat taliviyarākak koṇṭavi; taṉit taṉmi vāyntavi. ivviru kāviyaṅkaḷiṉ kālam ki.pi. 350- 450 ākalām eṉpar cuvelapil (1995). kāñcipurattil ki.pi. 5m nūṟṟāṇṭil vacitta tikñāṉar camskirutattil niyāyap piravēca eṉṉum aḷavi nūli eḻutiṉār. annūlukkum maṇimēkaliyiṉ cila pakutikaḷukkum neruṅkiya oppumi iruppatāl maṇimēkaliyiṉ kālatti ki.pi 5m nūṟṟāṇ ṭukkup piṉ taḷḷukiṟār viyāpuri. āṉāl maṇimēkaliyi āṅkilattil peyartta ālaṉ tāṉiyalō (Alin Danielou)  ikkarutti maṟuttu tamiḻar aḷavi nūṟkaruttukkaḷiyē
maṇimēkali āciriyarum, tikñāṉarum payaṉpaṭuttiyiruk kalām eṉṟum maṇimēkali ki.pi. iraṇṭām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉa tāka irukkalām eṉṟum karutukiṟār.

23. caṅka ilakkiyam paṟṟi virivāka ivviyalilum piṟkāla ilakkiyaṅkaḷil ciṟantavi paṟṟic curukkamākap piṉ iyalkaḷilum tantuḷḷataṟkuk kāraṇam uṇṭu. ṣelṭaṉ pōlak (2003) kūṟuvatu pōl oru nāṭṭiṉ (allatu oru nilapparappiṉ) taṉittaṉmiyiyē ataṉ moḻiyum ammoḻiyiṉ ilakkiyamumtāṉ nirṇayikkiṉṟaṉa eṉpatē atu. avar kūṟṟu varumāṟu: teṉ āciyā / intiyā / vaṅkāḷam ākiyavi muṟiyē teṉ āciya / intiya / vaṅkāḷi ilakkiyatti uruvākkiṉa eṉpatu evvaḷavu uṇmiyō avvaḷavu uṇmi: antanta ilakkiyaṅkaḷē antanta nilappakutikaḷi ākiyavaṟṟi uruvākkiṉa” eṉpatum ākum. oru nilappakutiyiṉ uṇarvukaḷip paṭampiṭittuk kāṭṭip parappuvataṉ mūlam appakutiyiṉ taṉittaṉmiyi ilakkiyamē uruvākkukiṟatu; oru nilappakutiyiṉ taṉmi, appakuti makkaḷiṉ nitarcaṉa vāḻkki ākiyaviyum appakutiyil uruvākum ilakkiyamum oṉṟukkoṉṟu tākkam viḷivippaviyākum.

Literature may have produced Bengal and India and South Asia as much as South Asia and India and Bengal have produced literature; that literary representation can conceptually organise space; and the dissemination of literary texts can turn that space into a lived reality, as much as space and lived realities condition conceptual organization and dissemination. p 27 of Pollock.

24. mēlē uḷḷa pattikaḷil kuṟitta aṟiñarkaḷiṉ tol kāppiyam / caṅka ilakkiyac cevviyal taṉmicciṟappu paṟṟiya āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷ varumāṟu; piṟamoḻiyāḷarukku ikkaruttuk kaḷi āṅkilattil terivikka vacatiyāka īṇṭu orē iṭattil tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa:-

As a source book not only of grammatical and literary study but also of human goography, social anthropology, culture, ecology and psychology, Tholkappiam contins very valuable data, and its importance for the study of Tamil culture, and of cultures in general, can hardly be exaggerated. However one must beware of taking the literary conventions as a kind of direct report, or even worse, as an objective explicit and ‘scientific’ treatment of ‘real life’. In some works it was assumed that the bardic court-poetry reflected directly and with utmost fidelity and realism the conduct of men and women of that age in love and war. But the poetry, based on conventions, reflects ‘real life’ only obliquely, since a literary convention was a pattern of fictional (or ‘ideal’) behaviour. The poets and the theoreticians had a clear understanding of the relationship between ‘Real life’ and its reflection in literature, since they clearly differentiated between the two: ulakiyal valakku denoted things sid and done in ‘real life’: Ceyyul valakku (or pulaneri valakku) denoted the practice in poetry of reflecting it. A literary convention is an agreement between writer and reader that certin themes will be represented in a certin way. The reader is thus truly able to appreciate the poet’s skill in handling a familiar theme by means of improvisation. The poetry of the ‘interior’ the subjective akam poetry is totally anonymous in the sense that akam poems never mention the hero or heroine by name, not in a single stanza. Hence they “embody what is typically human rather than what is merely individual and particular W.H.Hudson. An introduction to the study of literature, London. 1946.p97). The ‘objective’ external Puram poetry is partly impersonal. partly personal. Thus, e.g. the elegies are highly personal tributes to dead patrons and friends, genuine and spontaneous, expressing intimate and personal grief, “free from any conventional bucolic machinery”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1974) Tamil Literature (in series: A History
of Indian literature) Otto Harrassowitz. Wiesbaden. pp 35-36

“The discovery of this body of roughly 2300 verse (of Ettuthogi and Pathuppattu during 1880-1910) rised the Tamil language at one stroke from an insignificant neo-Indian Language to the standing of one of the great classical languages of the world. Whoever has made a more intimate acquaintance with this ancient poetry will readily subscribe to the assessment of the French Indologist Pierre Meille, when he states that this poetry is comparable with the choicest gems of old Greek lytical poetry, and that this ‘cycle of Sangam poetry’ represents one of the summits of literary creation in India and the whole world’
- K.V.Zvelebil (1960) NEW ORIENT (bi-monthly) 5, 1960pp

		3. reprinted in Tamil Culture x-2: April-June 1963.
		
		“The Dravidians, and in particular, the Tamils have contributed a great deal to the cultural richness of the world: Pallava and Chola temple architecture, Chola bronze sculpture, the dance form known as Bharatanatyam the so called Carnatic system of music. But proba bly the most significant contribution is that of Tamil literature. which still remins to be “discovered” and enjoyed by the non-Tamilians and adopted an an essential and remarkable part of universal heri tage”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1973) The smile of Murugan--on Tamil
literature of South India; Leiden: E.J.Brill. Preface

		“The early Tamil poetry was rather unique not only by virtue of the fact that some of its features were so unlike everything else in India, but by virtue of its literary excellence, those 26350 lines of poetry promote Tamil to the rank of one of the great classical languages of the world--though the world at large only just about begins to realise it.
 
“it is only the Tamil culture that has produced - uniquely so in India, an independent indigenous literary theory of a very high standard, including metrics and prosody, poetics and rhetoric”

		“there exist in India only two great specific and independent classical and historically attested cultures--the Sanskrit culture and the Tamil culture.”

“Tamil literature is the only Indian literature which is both classical and modern; while it shares antiquity with much of Sanskrit literature, and is as classical, in the best sense of the word as for example the ancient Greek poetry, it continues to be vigorously living modern writing of our days.
		
		“Classical Tamil literature is literature is about and of people. but not a Volksliterature”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1973) The smile of Murugan; pp 1, 4

“Life is condidered pleasent and joyful, death an inevitable end. There is no attempt to obtin release from life. Liberality and goodness are indulged in without motives of penance or recompense. About old age thereis a note of nostalgic resignation.

		“Life after death is represented as an abode of permanent happiness for the brave and good. and permanent suffering for the wicked. The happiness of future life is a reward of those who by their bravery and altruism established their glory (pukal) in this world. Love and courtship, marriage and children are considered necessary modes of personal perfection. If at all anything remins after death, it is the glory and honour, the prise due the memory of the days passed in heroism and in the service of fellow men.”
- K.V.Z. (1974) Tamil literature otto Harrassovitz, p.44.

“Ancient Tamil poetry is multivalent: it is so rich and comprehensive that it includes aesthetic structures and values which give high satisfaction to later ages and periods. It is conceived so that rather a community than a single individual could and can realize all its strata and systems. That is why it has survived generational tastes, and has acquired a permanent and universal position”
- K.V.Z (1974) ibid pp 35-36

In the depth and width of erotic experience and in the conceptual framework of the entire phenomenal world, perceived as akam and puram, Tamil culture is unique andoriginal. And as. for the parallels between Tamil and Sanskrit literature-why must it always be presumed that it was the Tamil works that borrowed from Aryan?”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1975) TAMIL LITERATURE (annals of Tamil
literature, abstining from value judgements) Leiden: E.J.Brill. p107


The ideal modern poem is expected to be objective, precise, organically complex and as well written as prose. Free from clichés an straddled adjectives, it should have the prose virtues of simplicity and hardness in a language charged with meaning to the utmost possible degree. xxxx A poem of indirection, yielding more than one layer of meaning, is a product of excellent craftsmanship. Tolka-- ppiyar’s insistence on the three aspects nokku, payan and eccam, is clearly indicative of his predilection for a poetry that works subtly, indirectly and sugge stively. Nokku demands every syllable, every word and every line to be in harmony with the poem as a whole. By payan is meant the implicit statement on the purpose of an object. Though eccam which is pf two types--colleccam and kurippeccam - interpreted in diverse ways by the commentators, it is used to avoid explicit complete statements and to exploit the suggestive potential of the minimum number of words employed in the poem.

		The concept of poetry as expressed in Tolhappiyar’s Porulathikaram and the practice of Sangam poets as evidenced in the still cherished anthologies would reveal that the ancient Tamils had a poetics of the lyric which Elder Olson was looking for but could not find in the well known languages of the world. (Elder Olson:1962 “Sailing to Byzantium; Prolegomena to a poetics of the lyric” in Willbur Scot (ed) FIVE APPROACHES OF LITERARY CRITICISM; London-Macmillan.)
- P. Marudanayagam; CHEMMOZHI I - 2 Jan-Mar 2007

“First, Tamil is of considerable antiquity. It predates the literature of other modern Indian languages by more than a thousand years. Its oldest work, the Tolkappiyam, contins parts that, judging from the earliest Tamil inscriptions, date back to about 200 BCE. The greatest works of ancient Tamil, the Sangam anthologies and the Pattupattu,
date to the first two centuries of the current era. They are the first great secular body of poetry written in India, predating Kalidasa’s works by two hundred years.

 
“Second, Tamil constitutes the only literary tradition indigenous to India that is not derived from Sanskrit. Indeed, its literature arose before the influence of Sanskrit in the South became strong and so is qualitatively different from anything we have in Sanskrit or other Indian languages. It has its own poetic theory, its own grammatical tradition, its own esthetics, and, above all, a large body of literature that is quite unique. It shows a sort of Indian sensibility that is quite different from anything in Sanskrit or other Indian languages, and it contins its own extremely rich and intellectual tradition.

“Third, the quality of classical Tamil literature is such that it is fit to stand beside the great literatures of Sanskrit, Greek, Latin, Chinese, Persian and Arabic. The subtlety and profundity of its works, their varied scope (Tamil is the only premodern Indian literature to treat the subaltern extensively), and their universality qualify Tamil to stand as one of the great classical traditions and literatures of the world. Everyone knows the Tirukkural, one of the world’s greatest works on ethics; but this is merely one of a myriad of major and extremely varied works that comprise the Tamil classical tradition. There is not a facet of human existence that is not explored and illuminated by this great literature.

“Finally, Tamil is one of the primary independent sources of modern Indian culture and tradition. I have written extensively on the influence of a Southern tradition on the Sanskrit poetic tradition. But equally important, the great sacred works of Tamil Hinduism, beginning with the Sangam Anthologies, have undergirded the development of modern Hinduis. Their ideas were taken into the Bhagavata Purana and other texts (in Telugu and Kannada as well as Sanskrit), whence they spread all over India. Tamil has its own works that are considered to be as sacred as the Vedas and that are recited alongside Vedic mantras in the great Vaishnava temples of South India (such as Tirupati). And just as Sanskrit is the source of the modern Indo- Aryan languages, classical Tamil is the source language of modern Tamil and Malayalam.

“The status of Tamil as one of the great classical languages of the world is something that is patently obvious to any one who knows the subjects” 
- George L. Hart (April 11, 2000)

		“Tamil is one of only a tiny number of languages in the world with a recorded history of more than two millennia. It has a central position in one of the world’s largest language familites, and no matter from where one enters on the study of comparative Dravidian, early acquaintance with Tamil, its history and its early grammars is imperative. It has a literary heritage which is unsurpassed the world over and fully deserving of the efforts of translation to make it widely known”
-R.E. Asher South Indian Horizons: Gros 70th Birth day

iyal 5
kaḷappirar kāla tamiḻakam (ki.pi. 300-600)

nam nāṭṭu varalāṟṟilum cari piṟanāṭṭu varalāṟukaḷilum cari cila nilappakutikaḷiṉ cila kāla varalāṟṟu nikaḻccikaḷ kuṟittu ōraḷavukkāvatu teḷivāka virivākak kūṟuvataṟkāṉa cāṉṟukaḷ kūṭak kiṭippatilli. akkālaṅkaḷic cilar iruṇṭa kālaṅkaḷ eṉpar. cila eṭuttukkāṭṭukaḷ varumāṟu:

i) ki.mu. 1700kkup piṉ cintuveḷit tol tamiḻa nākarikam ēṉ muṟṟilum aḻintatu? eppaṭi?
ii) cīṉāvil cu parampariyiṉar āṇṭa ki.mu. 900- 300 kālattil ciṟṟaracar mikappalar āṇṭaṉar. āṉāl vivaraṅkaḷ teriyavilli.
iii) vaṭaamerikkāvil meccikōvil ki.pi. 3-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil ciṟantu viḷaṅkiya mayā nākarikam ataṉ piṉ aṭiyōṭu aḻivuṟṟami.

tamiḻaka varalāṟṟil ki.pi. 300-600 kālattiyum attakiya iruṇṭa kālamākap palarum karutukiṉṟaṉar. kaḷappirar kālat tamiḻaka varalāṟṟip paṟṟi virivāṉa nūl eḻutiyuḷḷavarkaḷ irā. paṉṉīr celvam (1973); mayili cīṉi vēṅkaṭacāmi (1975); mu. aruṇācalam (1979); naṭaṉakācinātaṉ (1981) ākiyōr āvar.

2. caṅka ilakkiyaṅkaḷ kuṟittuḷḷa pāṇṭiya cōḻa cēra maṉṉaruḷ mikap piṟpaṭṭavarkaḷ kālam ki.pi. 250-300 aḷavil muṭivuṟṟatu. piṟkālap pāṇṭiyar āṭci ki.pi. 560-590il āṇṭa kaṭuṅkōṉ kālam mutal toṭaṅkiyatu. piṟkālac cōḻar āṭci vicayālayaṉ (ki.pi. 848-881) mutal toṭaṅkiyatu. pallava, pāṇṭiya maṉṉarkaḷ āḷukiyil cōḻanāṭu ki.pi. 848 varit toṭarntatāltāṉ cōḻa parampari ki.pi. 600 liruntē pāṇṭiyarip pōla mīṇṭum uyir peṟa iyalavilli.

3. caṅka kālac cēracōḻa pāṇṭiya maṉṉarkaḷ tammiṭiyē iṭiviṭātu pōriṭṭu valuviḻanta niliyil kaṉṉaṭa nāṭṭavarākiya ciravaṇa pukoḷa vaṭṭārattu kaḷippu nāṭṭuk kaḷappirar (=kaḷappāḷar) ēṟattāḻa ki.pi. 250l allatu ataṟkuc caṟṟup piṉṉar vantēṟikaḷāka vantu tamiḻakattik kippaṟṟi pāṇṭiya cōḻa cēra nāṭukaḷi ki.pi. 575 vari āṇṭaṉar eṉpar ma.cī. veṅkaṭacāmi. kē.ār. vēṅkaṭarāmaṉ (1956-57); ka.a. nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri, na. cuppiramaṇiyaṉ, paṉṉīrcelvam, kācinātaṉ, aruṇācalam ākiyōrum ikkaruttiṉarē. pāṇṭiya nāṭu muḻuvatiyumō cōḻanāṭu muḻuvatiyumō kaḷappirar muḻumiyākat tam āṭcikkuk koṇṭu vantatākak kūṟa iyalātu eṉpar aruṇācalam. kaḷappirar kaṉṉaṭar eṉpataṟkuc cāṉṟukaḷ varumāṟu.

		‘kāṉak kaṭicūḻvaṭukak karunāṭar maṉṉaṉ’
- tiruttoṇṭar purāṇam
mūrtti nāyaṉār.
		“maturi vavviya karunaṭar vēntaṉ”
- kallāṭam

4. pi.ji.el. cuvāmi (1976) karuttu “kaḷappirar paṭi yeṭuppu” eṉpatu ki.pi. 8 ām nūṟṟāṇṭil (kaṉṉaṭiya) kaṅka aracarkaḷuṭiya orupaṭi tiṇṭukkallukkum maturikkum iṭippaṭṭatāṉa pāṇṭiya nāṭṭiṉ oru ciṟu nilap pakutiyik kippaṟṟi oru cila āṇṭukaḷ āṇṭatik kuṟippatutāṉ eṉpatākum! akkarutti aruṇācalam maṟuttuḷḷār. kaḷappirar tamiḻarē eṉṟu karutiya es. kiruṣṇacāmi aiyaṅkār, mu. irākaviyaṅkār (1930); pi.ṭi. cīṉivāca aiyaṅkār (1929) ka.pa. aṟavāṇaṉ (1974) mutaliyavarkaḷ karuttum iṉṟu ēṟkappaṭuvatilli.

5. kaḷappirar araca kulattavar allar; koḷḷi tēṭi vanta iṉakkuḻuviṉarē (merely a sort of maruding tribe)eṉpar aruṇācalam. kaṉṉaṭa nāṭṭiliruntu vanta kaḷappiraruḷ cāmāṉiyar pēciyatu paḻaṅkaṉṉaṭamāka iruntirukkum (atuvum ki.pi. mūṉṟām nūṟṟāṇṭil irunta - akkālat tamiḻiliruntu atika vēṟupāṭu illāta paḻaṅkaṉṉaṭamāka iruntirukkum). kaḷappiraruḷ āḷum varkkattiṉar camaṇar. camaṇattuṟavikaḷum paṭittavarkaḷum arttamākati moḻiyip payaṉpaṭuttiya tākat terikiṟatu. eṉavētāṉ ñāṉa campantar tēvārattil (3:39:4)

“manti pōl tirintu āriyattoṭu centamiḻp
payaṉ aṟikilā antakar”

eṉṟu camaṇarip paḻikkiṟār.

6. tamiḻakattil kaḷappirar ki ōṅki irunta kālattil paḻiya cēra, cōḻa, pāṇṭiya vēntar parampariyiṉar avarkaḷukkuk kīḻ aṭaṅkic ciṟṟaracarkaḷāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpar vēṅkaṭacāmi. kaḷappira maṉṉarkaḷ peyarkaḷ teriyavilli. ki.pi. 10ām nūṟṟāṇṭu nūlākiya yāpparuṅkala viruttiyuri mēṟkōḷ veṇpā oṉṟu kaḷappāḷaṉ pōr veṟṟiyip pukaḻkiṟatu. atē virutti  yuriyāṉatu accuta kaḷappāḷa aracaṉ cēra cōḻa pāṇṭiyavēntarkaḷit taḷiyiṭṭapōtu avaṉip pāṭiyaṉavāka nāṉku veṇpākkaḷiyum tarukiṟatu. kaḷappira maṉṉar aṉivarum accutaṉ eṉṉum potup peyar tarittaṉar. ki.pi. 5ām nūṟṟāṇṭil cōḻa nāṭṭup pūtamaṅkalattil putta vikāriyil taṅkiyirunta poḻutu putta tatta tērar viṉaya viniccayam nūli eḻutiṉār. annūlum “kaḷappa (kaḷappira) kulattiṟ piṟanta accuta vikkantaṉik” kuṟikkiṟatu. kaḷappira aracar aṉivarumē viṇavap peyarāṉa accutaṉ eṉṉum ciṟappup peyarik koṇṭiruntaṉar eṉṟum ma.cī.vē. karutukiṟār. eṉiṉum kaḷappira aracar palar camaṇarākavum iruntuḷḷaṉar.

7. akkālattil ilaṅkic ciṅkaḷa vēntar aracil niliyaṟṟa taṉmi iruntatu eṉpati cūlavamcam kāṭṭukiṟatu. ki.pi. 436-463 āṇṭukaḷil ilaṅki aracip pāṇṭiyar parampariyil vanta pāṇṭu (= pāṇṭiyaṉ) 436-441 eṉpavaṉ kippaṟṟi avaṉum avaṉ makaṉkaḷ iruvarum (441-460) āṇṭaṉar eṉa cūlavamcam kūṟukiṟatu. kaḷappirarukku aṭaṅkiyirunta pāṇṭiya maṉṉar kulattic cārnta oruvaṉē anta pāṇṭu āka irukkalām eṉpar ma.cī. vē (annūl iyal 3). ki.pi 494-515il āṇṭa ciṅkaḷa maṉṉaṉ mokkallānaṉ I. avaṉukku muṉṉar āṇṭa kassapaṉ I avaṉit turattiya taṉāl mokkallānaṉ tamiḻnāṭṭil aṭikkalam pukuntiruntatākavum appoḻutu nikantarkaḷ (=camaṇar) uṭaṉ toṭarpu koṇṭu ilaṅki ceṉṟu kassapaṉi veṉṟa tākavum cūlavamcam kūṟukiṟatu. anta nikantarkaḷ tamiḻakak kaḷappira aracarāka irukka vēṇṭum eṉpar ma.cī.vē.

8. accuta vikkantaṉ kālattil uṟiyūril iruntu cōḻanāṭṭiṉ oru ciṟupakutiyi āṇṭu vanta cōḻaṉ ceṅkaṇāṉ (ivaṉ puṟanāṉūṟṟil varum ceṅkaṇāṉ allaṉ; pinnāḷil appeyar koṇṭu āṇṭavaṉ “eṇtōḷīcarkku eḻilmāṭam eḻupatu ceytāṉ” eṉpar aruṇācalam.) ivaṉit tirumaṅkiyāḻvār pārāṭṭukiṟār. kaḷappirar ātikka kālattil cōḻa araca kulattiṉar cilar āntira nāṭṭukkuc ceṉṟu innāḷ kaṭappi karnūl pakutikaḷil “irēṉāṭṭuc cōḻar” eṉṟa peyaril pala nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ āṇṭu vantaṉar. teluṅkil “cōḻar” eṉpatu “cōṭar” eṉa māṟiviṭṭatu. itu vēṅkaṭacāmi, aruṇācalam iruvar karuttum ākum.

9. cuntarar tiruttoṇṭat toki yil “kūṟṟaṉ kaḷantikkōṉ” eṉṟu aḻikkum maṉṉaṉi nampi yāṇṭār nampi tiruttoṇṭar antāti yil ‘kaḷappāḷaṉ
ākiya kūṟṟuvaṉ’ eṉa aḻikkiṟār; (kaḷanti iṉṟu tiruttuṟippūṇṭi arukē uḷḷa kaḷappāl ākum.) vēṅkaṭacāmi pōṉṟōr kūṟṟuvanāyaṉārik kaḷappāḷar kulattavar eṉṟu colvataṟku ātāramilli. kaḷantiyi ‘kaḷappu’ eṉat tirintu ‘kaḷappāḷaṉ’ = kaḷantiyi āṇṭavaṉ eṉṟu uṉṉittuk kūṟiṉar. kūṟṟuvar kaḷappāḷa kulattavar eṉa cuntararum kūṟavilli; nampiyum kūṟavilli. ākavē kūṟṟuva
nāyaṉār kaḷappirar allar, eṉpar aruṇācalam.

1 0 . pi ṟkāla t ti l tamiḻaka va ralāṟṟi l varu m kaḷappāḷarāyar, neṟkuṉṟam kiḻār, accuta kaḷappāḷarāyar (civañāṉapōtam eḻutiya meykaṇṭār tanti) 13ām nūṟṟāṇṭu cārnta cila kaḷappāḷarāyarkaḷ ākiyōrum kaḷappāḷar kulattavar allar. kaḷappirar / kaḷappāḷari ki.pi. 575kkup piṉ aṭakka utaviya “tamiḻc cātiyiṉarē” kaḷappāḷarāyar eṉappaṭṭavarāvar eṉpatu aruṇācalam karuttu.

11. ki.pi. 575ai oṭṭi kaḷappirarimiṭaruntu pāṇṭiya nāṭṭip pāṇṭiyaṉ kaṭuṅkōṉ mīṇṭum kippaṟṟiṉāṉ.

“aḷavariya ātirājari akalanīkki akaliṭattik
kaḷapraṉeṉṉuṅ kali aricaṉ kikkoṇṭataṉi
iṟakkiyapiṉ
.............. kaṭuṅkōṉeṉṉuṅ katirvēṟṟeṉṉaṉ”

- parāntaka neṭuñcaṭiyaṉ (ki.pi. 756-815) uṭiya kēḷvikkuṭi ceppēṭu.

“kaḷappāḻarik kaḷikaṭṭa maṟṟiraṇṭōḷ
mākkaṭuṅ kōṉ” 		
- parāntaka vīranārāyaṇaṉ (860-905) taḷavāypuram ceppēṭu.

		taṅkaḷik kaḷappirar tōṟkaṭitta veṟuppil pāṇṭiyar aṉṉārik kaliyaricar (tīya aracar) eṉa ikkalveṭṭil paḻittaṉar pōlum; mēlum kaḷappirarukku
camaṇa - puttarkaḷōṭu irunta toṭarpukaḷit tamiḻaka varalāṟṟāciriyar mikippaṭuttikkūṟukiṉṟaṉar. eṉavētāṉ kaḷappirar “tīya aracar” ākkappaṭṭuviṭṭaṉar eṉpar parṭaṉ sṭeyṉ. camaṇamata untutalāl tāṉ paṇṭit tamiḻ. vēntarik kaḷappirar veṉṟaṉar; pirāmaṇiya niṟuvaṉaṅkaḷukku ikkaṭṭu ēṟpaṭṭatu eṉṟu nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri kūṟuvataṟku ātāram illi eṉpar sṭeyṉ. ‘All the Kings’s Mana: perspectives on medieval South India’ July 1974 Seminar paper published in J.F. Richard (Kingship and authority in South Asia; University of Wisconsin; Madison 1978.)

ki.pi. 575ai oṭṭi kaḷappirariṭamiruntu cōḻa nāṭṭip pallava cimmaviṣṇu kippaṟṟik koṇṭāṉ.

12. piṟkālattil muttariyar eṉṟu vaḻaṅkiya kuṟunila maṉṉarum kaḷappirarum potuvāṉa oru kuṭiyiliruntu pirintu vaḷarntavar eṉpār naṭaṉa kācinātaṉ (1976) kaḷappirar camayam

13. kaḷappirar camayam perumpālum camaṇam; cōḻa nāṭṭik kippaṟṟiyavarkaḷ maṭṭum puttam eṉa vēṅkaṭacāmiyum aruṇācalamum kāṭṭum ātāraṅkaḷ maṟukkak kūṭiyaviyākat tōṉṟavilli. tiruñāṉa campantarum tirunāvukkaracarum - kuṟippāka muṉṉavar – camaṇa putta camayaṅkaḷit tam tēvārap patikaṅkaḷil kaṭumiyāṉa vacavu moḻikaḷāl tākkuvatum, camaṇarik ki.pi. 6 ām nūṟṟāṇṭil kaḻuvēṟṟiya ceytikaḷi cēkkiḻārum piṟarum cātaṉiyāka makiḻcciyuṭaṉ terivippatum mukkiyamāṉa ātāramākum.

14. parṭaṉ sṭeyṉ, 1978 āyvukkaṭṭuriyil kūṟum maṟṟoru karuttu; ‘kaḷappirar ātikkam muṭintavuṭaṉ tamiḻnāṭṭil camaṇa matattiṉ kati muṭintu viṭṭatu pōla tamiḻnāṭṭiṉarāṉa varalāṟṟāciriyar cuḷuvākak karuti viṭukiṉṟaṉar, makēntiravarmaṉ pāṇṭiya vēntaṉ ākiya perumaṉṉarkaḷuṭiya matamāka irunta ataṉi avvaḷavu virivāka oḻittuk kaṭṭiyirukka muṭiyātu; aracavikaḷil celvākku pōyviṭṭālum vēṟu pala tuṟikaḷilum (paṇpāṭu ilakkiyam uṭpaṭa) tamiḻnāṭṭil camaṇac celvākku mēlum cila nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ valuvāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum... āntira, karunāṭakap pakutikaḷil accelvākku mēlum cila nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ nīṭittatu’ eṉpatākum.

toṇṭi nāṭṭil pallavar

15. ki.pi. 300-600 kāla aḷavil tamiḻakattil toṇṭi nāṭu pallavarkaḷ āṭciyiṉ kīḻ iruntatu. intak kāla aḷavil avarkaḷ mutalil (āntira cātavākaṉarippōla) pirākirutattilum piṉṉar camskirutattilum cila ceppēṭukaḷi
veḷiyiṭṭuḷḷaṉar. akkālap pallava aracarkaḷ varalāṟṟi eḻutappōtumāṉa cāṉṟukaḷ acceppēṭukaḷil illi. pallava araca parampari tōṟṟam kuṟittu vēṟupaṭṭa karuttukaḷ
uḷḷaṉa:

cākar allatu pahlavar - pi.ṭi. cīṉivāca aiyaṅkār intiyar - kē.pi. aiyasvāl, nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri nākar - em. cīṉivāca aiyaṅkār

	āntirap pakutiyiṉar - ār. kōpālaṉ; ci. mīṉāṭci, ṭi.vi. makāliṅkam, oy. cupparāyalu.

	tamiḻar [(kurumpar/tiriyar (toṇṭiyar)] - irā. rākaviyaṅkār, rāca nāyakam, ār. cattiya nātiyar; eṉ. cuppiramaṇiyaṉ (ivarkaḷ karuttuppaṭi tiriyar eṉṉum tamiḻa marapiṉar vēṅkaṭattukku appāl āṭci niṟuvi, āntira cātavākaṉarukkuk kīḻ cila kālam āṭci ceytu viṭṭu, piṉṉar mīṇṭum taṅkaḷ toṉmiyakamāṉa toṇṭi nāṭṭukku vantu viṭṭaṉar eṉpatākum.)

camuttira kuptaṉ taṉatu alakapāt tūṇ kalveṭṭil (ki.pi. 350il) kiruṣṇā āṟṟaṅkariyil tāṉ pōril veṉṟa maṉṉarkaḷil oruvaṉāka “kāñci viṣṇu kōpaṉi”k kuṟippiṭukiṟāṉ. pallavar ceppēṭukaḷ kuṟippiṭum pallava maṉṉarkaḷil viṣṇukōpaṉum oruvaṉ. (ki.pi 600-900 kāla aḷavil pallavar āṭci kuṟittu aṭutta iyalil kāṇka.)

kaḷappirar kālattil tamiḻ ilakkiyam, kalikaḷ

16. kaḷappirar kālattil camaṇamum puttamum araciṉ ātaravuṭaṉ vaḷarntirukka vēṇṭum. eṉavē ki.pi. āṟām nūṟṟāṇṭil ivviru camayaṅkaḷi oḻikka ñāṉa campantar, nāvukkaracar pōṉṟōr pala koṭumikaḷic ceyya vēṇṭiyatāyiṟṟu. kaḷappirar kaṉṉaṭar ākiyāl avarkaḷ tamiḻ moḻikkum ilakkiyattiṟkum ākkam aḷittatākat teriyavilli; eṉiṉum avarkaḷ āṭcik kālattilum tamiḻar moḻiyiyum, ilakkiyattiyum nalla muṟiyil vaḷart tārkaḷ eṉṟu ma.cī.vē. karutukiṟār. akkālattil eḻunta mutaṉmiyāṉa tamiḻ nūlkaḷ eṉa avar kūṟuvatu:

ki.pi. nūṟṟāṇṭu
patiṉeṇ kīḻkkaṇakku nūlkaḷil 
(ki.pi. 250kku muṟpaṭṭa tirukkuṟaḷ,
kaḷavaḻi nāṟpatu, mutumoḻik kāñci						3-6
ākiya mūṉṟum nālaṭiyārum tavira
ēṉiya 14m)

nālaṭiyār										7

avinayam, kākki pāṭiṉiyam							3-6

cīvaka cintāmaṇi 									5-6

koṅkuvēḷ picāca moḻiyiliruntu peyarttu eḻutiya
peruṅkati 										5-6

kārikkālammiyār nūlkaḷ 							”

iṟiyaṉār kaḷaviyal 								”	

muttoḷḷāyiramum tirumantiramum ikkālattaṉavē eṉpar paṇṭārattār. “pārppaṉar tamatu mēmpāṭṭi iḻantaṉar eṉpatāltāṉ (kaḷappirar) kālatti iruṇṭakālam eṉṟu colkiṉṟaṉar” eṉṟu mu.rā. perumāḷ mutaliyār (1985) kaṭṭuriyil kūṟukiṟār.

17. ki.pi. 600kkup piṉṉar civa viṇava camayaṅkaḷ taḻitta poḻutu kaḷappirar kālattiya camaṇa, putta camayam cārnta tamiḻ nūlkaḷ pala aḻikkappaṭṭirukka vēṇṭum eṉpar vēṅkaṭacāmi. tiruñāṉa campantar camaṇaruṭaṉ nikaḻttiya aṉalvāta, puṉalvātac ceytikaḷ itik kuṟippaṉavēyeṉka. ēṟkeṉavē itupaṟṟi cārlas ī.kōvar mutaliyavarkaḷ terivittuḷḷa karuttukkaḷi caṅka ilakkiyam paṟṟiya pakutiyil kaṇṭōm. 

18. kaḷappirar kālattil puṟapporuḷ cārntum akapporuḷ cārntum caṅka kālattiliruntu māṟupaṭṭa putiya karuttukaḷ uruvāyiṉa. pōril pakivarik koṉṟu peṟum veṟṟiyi viṭa aimpulaṉkaḷ, kāmam, vekuḷi mutaliya akappakikaḷi (arukar, puttar pōla) velvatē ciṟanta veṟṟi eṉṟu putuk karuttu camaṇa putta camayaṅkaḷāl uruvāyiṟṟu. civa viṇavarkaḷō māntar akavoḻuk kattiviṭa paktarkaḷ ‘nāyaki - nāyaka pāvattil’ kaṭavuḷi vaḻipaṭa mēṟkoḷḷum teyvīkak kātalē ciṟantatu eṉṟa putukkarutti uruvākkiṉar. itaṟku vaki ceyyavē iṟiyaṉār akapporuḷ / kaḷaviyal eṉṉum putu ilakkaṇa nūli eḻutiṉar. akkaḷaviyal paṟṟiyum ataṟku caṅka kālattukkup pintiya iraṇṭām nakkīrar uruvākkiya uri paṟṟiyum katikaḷ uruvākkappaṭṭaṉa. ikkaḷaviyalum ataṉ uriyum ipputiya karutti uruvākkiya piṉṉar ki.pi. 7ām nūṟṟāṇṭil appar, campantar, āḻvārkaḷ pōṉṟōr akapporuḷ tuṟiyaminta paktippāṭalkaḷi iyaṟṟiṉar. itu ma.cī.vē. muṭivu ākum.

19. kaḷappirar kālattil tamiḻ moḻiyum ilakkiyamum tākkuṇṭaṉa; piṉṉar ki.pi. āṟām nūṟṟāṇṭil civa camayattiṉ ki ōṅkiya piṉṉarē tamiḻ paḻiya niliyip peṟṟatu eṉpatu aruṇācalattiṉ nili ākum! avarukku muṉṉar itu paṟṟi eḻutiya catāciva paṇṭārattār, oḷavi turicāmippiḷḷi pōṉṟavarkaḷ karuttum ituvē. potuvāka varalāṟu paṟṟiya aṟiñarkaḷiṉ karuttukaḷik (mēlē iyal 1) kāṇpavarkaḷukku ivviru vakiyiṉariṉ muraṇpaṭṭa nili viyappit tarātu. ici, nāṭakam, mutaliyaṉa vāḻntaṉa, vaḷarntaṉa eṉpār vēṅkaṭacāmi; vīḻntaṉa
eṉpār aruṇācalam!

20. kaḷappirar āṭci nallatā, keṭṭatā eṉṟa veṭṭu oṉṟu tuṇṭu iraṇṭākak kūṟavilliyē eṉak karutalām! ittakiya cikkalāṉa poruṇmikaḷil appaṭik kūṟa
iyalātu; kūṟuvatum cariyalla eṉpatē ciṟanta varalāṟṟaṟiñar karuttu ākum. ilaṭcakkaṇakkāṉa āvaṇaṅkaḷum pala āyirakkaṇakkāṉa ātāra nūlkaḷum 1789 pireñcup puraṭci paṟṟi irukkiṉṟaṉa. iruntum appuraṭciyiṉ tōṟṟam, viḷivukaḷip paṟṟi iṉṟiya niliyilum kūṭak kaṟārāṉa muṭivukaḷik kūṟa iyalātu eṉpar varalāṟṟaṟiñar ji.em. ṭirevelyaṉ.

“ittakiya mika mukkiyamāṉa kaṭuñcikkalāṉa viṣayaṅkaḷip paṟṟi “itu tāṉ varalāṟṟiṉ tīrppu” eṉṟu entak kālattilum kaṟārākak kūṟiviṭa iyalātu”

“ittakiya viṣayaṅkaḷil kiṭittuḷḷa varalāṟṟuc cāṉṟukaḷiṉ aṭippaṭiyil avaravar tamakkut tāmē cintittu ōraḷavukku oru muṭivukku vara iyalum. appaṭic cintittu muṭivukku vara muyalvatu namatu cintaṉiyāṟṟalukkuc ciṟanta payiṟciyākum; nam aṉivarukkum iṉṟiyamiyāta payiṟciyum kūṭa.”

		“varalāṟṟip payilvataṉ mūlam mānta iṉattiṉ aṉittup piracṉikaḷiyum akaṉṟa nuṇṇiya pārviyuṭaṉum amitiyākavum cintittu muṭivu ceyyum tiṟaṉum vaḻakkamum namakku varum. paṇṭiya varalāṟṟuc ceytikaḷi ellāk kōṇaṅkaḷiliruntum amitiyāka eṇṇip pārttu eṭi pōṭum poḻutu, maṉam namatu aṉṟāṭap piracciṉikaḷik kūrnta nuṇṇaṟivuṭaṉ purintu koḷḷum tiṟaṉi eytum. mānta iṉattiṉ iṭiyāppac cikkalkaḷic ceytittāḷ, tirippaṭam [ippoḻutu, ivaṟṟiyum viṭa mika mōcamāṉa tollik kāṭciyiyum cērttuk koḷḷalām] ākiya pullaṟivāṇmimikka ūṭakaṅkaḷ arivēkkāṭṭut taṉamāka (ēṉ 1/100) vēkkāṭṭut taṉamākak kūṭa) “eḷimippaṭutti” parappuri ceytu takiṭutattam paṇṇuvatiliruntu nammik kāttuk koḷḷa attiṟaṉ utavum.”

		“On such great and complex issues, there can never be a final verdict of history. But at least it is possible to have an opinion of some value. And the attempt to form such opinion in all the historical light now avilable, is an education to the mind, the sort of education we all most terribly need.”

		‘But, if we calmly study the past from as many angles as possible, we shall all of us gin in wisdom and understanding. We shall acquire a mentality which, when we return to our own problems, will be less at the mercy of newspapers and films. trying to make us take short cuts to truth and to oversimplify the tangled skein of human affairs.”
- G.M. Trevelyan: An autobiography and other essays.

kiruṣṇacāmi, a. 1990 tamiḻnāṭṭu varalāṟu pallavar,
pāṇṭiyar kālam (ki.pi. 500-900) eṉṉum nūlil
kaḷappirar paṟṟiya pakuti; tamiḻnāṭu aracu tamiḻ vaḷarcci
iyakkakam.

iyal 6
pāṇṭiyar - pallavar āṭci
ki.pi.7-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ

“kaḷappāḷarik kaḷikaṭṭa” (taḷavāypuram ceppēṭu) kaṭuṅkōṉ pāṇṭiya araci mīṇṭum niṟuvi ki.pi. 570-590 āṇṭukaḷil kōlōcciṉāṉ. ivaṉ vaḻit tōṉṟalkaḷāka ki.pi. 7-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil āṇṭa pāṇṭiyarkaḷ koṭivaḻi paṟṟiyum āṭciyāṇṭukaḷ paṟṟi aṟiñarkaḷiṭiyē karuttu vēṟupāṭu uṇṭu. avarkaḷ koṭivaḻiyi kē.vi. irāmaṉ (1977) piṉvarumāṟu tantuḷḷār. (atu paṇṭārattār (1956) koṭivaḻiyiliruntu caṟṟē māṟupaṭukiṟatu.)

kaṭuṅkōṉ (575-600)

māṟavarmaṉ avaṉicūḷāmaṇi (600-620)

ceḻiyaṉ cēntaṉ (620-642)
(ivaṉ tirunelvēli māvaṭṭam maliyaṭik
kuṟicci kukik kōyili amittavaṉ)

māṟavarmaṉ arikēcari (642-700)
(tiruñāṉacampantar kālam)

kōccaṭiyaṉ raṇatīraṉ (700-768)
(cuntarar kālam)

māṟavarmaṉ arikēcari rācacimmaṉ I
(nammāḻvār kālam)

	neṭuñcaṭiyaṉ parāntakaṉ (varakuṇaṉ I) 768-815 (caṅkarāccāriyār kālam) (vēḷvikkuṭi, cīvaramaṅkalam, ciṉṉamaṉūr (ciṟiyatu) ceppēṭukaḷ neṭuñcaṭiyaṉ kālattavi. neṭuñcaṭiyaṉ parāntakaṉ vēṟu; varakuṇaṉ I

		vēṟu eṉpār paṇṭārattār. parāntakaṉukkum cirivallapaṉukkum iṭiyil irācacimmaṉ II, (varakuṇaṉ I eṉa iruvar āṇṭirukka vēṇṭum eṉpār paṇṭārattār.)

(teḷḷāṟu pōril pallavaṉ nantivarmaṉ

(tiruppuṟampayam pōril cōḻaṉ 			taḷavāypuram ceppēṭu

									rāca cimmaṉ II - 900-920
							(paṇṭārattār karuttuppaṭi irācacimmaṉ III)

2. mutalām varakuṇaṉiṉ (768-815) taḷavāypuram
ceppēṭṭiliruntu āci nāṭṭut tirumaṅkalattik kaṭuṅkōṉ
piramatāyamākak koṭuttāṉ eṉṟu aṟikiṟōm. arikēcari
māṟavarmaṉ (642-700) hiraṇayakarpamum tulāpāramum
ceytāṉ. iṟiyaṉār akapporuḷ uriyil eṭuttuk
kāṭṭākat tarappaṭṭuḷḷa pāṇṭikkōvi nūliṉ
pāṭṭuṭit talivaṉ ivaṉē eṉpar. māṟavarmaṉ irāca
cimmaṉ ai cōḻa nāṭṭiṉ perumpakutiyiyum āṇṭatākat
terikiṟatu. parāntaka neṭuñcaṭiyaṉ (768-815) uṭiya
vēḷvikkuṭic ceppēṭṭip paṟṟi muṉ iyalil kaṇṭōm.
vēlaṅkuṭi eṉṉum ūrip paṇṭittaṉ paḻampeyar nīkki
srīvaramaṅkalam eṉap peyariṭṭu piramatāya kirāmamākki
cujjāta paṭṭaṉukkut tāṉam koṭuttavaṉum ivaṉē.
ivvāṟu pirāmaṇarkaḷukku piramatāyamāka nilam koṭutta
pāṇṭiyar ceppēṭukaḷiṉ camskirutap pakutikaḷil piṉvarum
vācakaṅkaḷ uḷḷaṉa:

“pirāmaṇarkaḷukkut tāṉam ceytatik kāppatik
kāṭṭilum puṇṇiyattiṟkuk kāraṇamāṉatu vēṟetuvum
ippūmiyil illi. ati apakarippatik kāṭṭilum
pāvattiṟkuk kāraṇamāṉatu ippūvulakil etuvum illi.
		*******

“viṣam eṉṟu collappaṭuvatu viṣam alla.
pirāmaṇaṉ cottuttāṉ viṣam. viṣam oruvaṉik
kollum. pirāmaṇaṉ cottu (apakarippavaṉuṭiya) puttira
puttirarkaḷi aḻikkum.”

“pirāmaṇarkkut tāṉ aḷittatum piṟar aḷittatumāṉa
pūmiyi apakarippavaṉ 60,000 āṇṭukaḷ malappuḻuvākap
piṟantu uḻalvāṉ” 
-ciṉmiṉūrc ciṟiya ceppēṭu

3. srīmāṟa cirivallapaṉ (815-862) ilaṅki mītu oru taṭavi paṭi eṭuttu veṉṟāṉ. ivaṉ kālattil vaṭakkil pallavarkaḷuṭaṉ mōti, teḷḷāṟṟil pallava nantivarmaṉ III iṭam tōṟṟāṉ. aṭuttu vanta varakuṇaṉ II (862-885) tiruppuṟampiyattil pallavaṉ aparājitaṉ, kaṅkaṉ pirutivipati I, cōḻaṉ ātittaṉ I (vicayālayaṉ makaṉ) ākiyōriṭam tōṟṟataṉāl cōḻa nāṭṭiṉ teṉpakutiyil atuvari iruntu vanta pāṇṭiyarāṭci appakutiyil muṭivukku vantatu. avaṉ tampi parāntaka vīranārāyaṇaṉ tantatē taḷavāypuram ceppēṭu. “maturi koṇṭa” cōḻaṉ parāntakaṉ ai (905-955) pāṇṭiyaṉ irācacimmaṉ II (900-920)yi veṉṟu pāṇṭiya nāṭṭik kippaṟṟiṉāṉ. irācacimmaṉukku ilaṅki aracaṉ paṭi aṉuppi utaviyum payaṉ illi. ki.pi. 920liruntu pāṇṭiyanāṭu cōḻaṉ parāntakaṉ I āṭciyiṉ kīḻvantu viṭṭatu. kaṭuṅkōṉ parampari ittuṭaṉ vīḻnta piṉṉar pāṇṭiya araca parampari mīṇṭum tuḷirttu valuppeṟṟu 13ām nūṟṟāṇṭil tamiḻakattil perum pakutiyik kaṭiciyākak kuṟukiya kālam āṇṭu attuṭaṉ muṭivuṟṟatu (iyal 8)

4. ki.pi. 7ām nūṟṟāṇṭiliruntu civa nāyaṉmārkaḷ, viṣṇava āḻvārkaḷ talimiyil pirāmaṇiya matam valuppeṟṟatu. nāyaṉmārkaḷil mutaliṭam vakikkum tiruñāṉacampantar, tirunāvukkarar, cuntarar, māṇikkavācakar ākiyōr ikkāla kaṭṭattic cārntavarkaḷ. māṟavarmaṉ arikēcari (642-700) kālattil ñāṉacampantaṉ camaṇa muṉivarkaḷi aṉalvātam, puṉalvātam mutaliya “aṟputaṅkaḷ” ceytu veṉṟu avarkaḷik kaḻuvēṟṟiṉāṉ eṉṟu periya purāṇam mutaliyaṉa kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. ataṉpiṉ pāṇṭiya nāṭṭil perumaḷavil irunta camaṇarkaḷ, avarkaḷ kōyilkaḷ, pāḻikaḷ ākiyavaṟṟic campantaṉ aḻittatip piṉvarumāṟu periyapurāṇam koṇṭāṭukiṟatu :-

pūḻiyaṉ maturi uḷḷār puṟattuḷār amaṇar cērum
pāḻiyum arukar mēvum paḷḷiyum āṉa ellām
kīḻuṟap paṟittup pōkkik kiḷaroḷit tūymi ceytē”

campantaṉ patikaṅkaḷ 383 uḷḷaṉa, ovvoṉṟilum 11 pāṭalkaḷuṭaṉ. ovvoru patikattilum pattām pāṭalil camaṇa puttar mītu kaṭumiyāṉa vacavu moḻikaḷ vīcappaṭukiṉṟaṉa - kāraṇa kāriya viḷakkamō, tarukkavātamō iṉṟi. ivaṟṟi aṟiñar va.cu. ceṅkalvarāyapiḷḷi (1946), tamatutēvāra oḷineṟi: campantar - ārāyccip pakuti pakkaṅkaḷ 189-230 il tokuttu tantuḷḷār. cila coṟṟoṭarkaḷ varumāṟu: “aṟivilāta vaṉ camaṇar”, “kuṇṭarām amaṇar”, “puttar camaṇuritta poytaṉi uttamam eṉak koḷātu”. vēta neṟiyic camaṇa puttar kaṭippiṭikkātatik kaṭumiyākat tākkukiṟār.

vēṭṭu vēḷvi ceyyāta amaṇki (315. 1)
vētaneṟi aṟikilātār (331.10)
vētavēḷviyi nintaṉi ceytuḻal ātamil
amaṇoṭu (366.1)

5. nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri 1929il eḻutiya pāṇṭiyar varalāṟṟukku B.S.O.A.S  (1930-2) itaḻil matippuri eḻutiya jē. cārpeṉṭiyar, cāttiri tamatu nūlil camaṇap paṭukoliyip pūci meḻukuvatip piṉvarumāṟu kaṇṭittār. “tamiḻakattil akkālattil perumaḷavukku piṟacamaya veṟuppum piṟacamayattiṉar oṟuppum nikaḻntati cāttiriyār kaṇṭum kāṇāmalum viṭṭuviṭukiṟār. ki.pi. 7ām nūṟṟāṇṭil pāṇṭiya nāṭṭil camaṇar koṭumiyāka oṟukkappaṭṭaṉar eṉpatil aiyamilli”. tamatu teṉṉintiya varalāṟu (IV Edn 1976) nūlilum cāttiri camaṇar kaḻuvēṟṟap paṭṭati nampattakkataṉṟu, puṉikati eṉṟu otukkukiṟār. cāttiriyiṉ icceyali cuvirā jeysvāl (1974)m parṭaṉ sṭeyṉ - um kaṇṭittuḷḷaṉar. sṭeyiṉ tamatu intiya varalāṟu, 1998il camaṇarik kaḻuvēṟṟiyataṟku campantaṉē kāraṇam eṉṟa ceppiyuḷḷār. (“Sambandhar may even have convinced the king to complete his conversion by impaling the heads of 8000 Jaina teachers, an event long celebrated at the major Siva temple of Madura” p. 134) 8000 camaṇarik koṉṟati cēkkiḻār, nampiyāṇṭār nampi, oṭṭakkūttaṉ pōṉṟōr pukaḻntuḷḷaṉar. camaṇarik kaḻuvēṟṟiyatik kāṭṭum ciṟpaṅkaḷ cila kōyilkaḷil iṉṟum uḷḷati ma.cī.vē. tamatu camaṇamum tamiḻum, 1954 nūlil kuṟippiṭṭu vēṟu pala ātāraṅkaḷiyum tantuḷḷār. poruḷātārattil kiraṣām viti kūṟuvatu “nalla nāṇayattuṭaṉ tiruṭṭu nāṇayam puḻakkattil viṭappaṭumpoḻutu tiruṭṭukkācē virintu paravalākac celāvaṇiyākum; nalla nāṇayam otuṅkiviṭum Bad money drives out good money” eṉpatākum. camūkam – paṇpāṭu ivi paṟṟiya māntaviyal maṟṟum varalāṟṟiliruntu nām “itu pōṉṟa kiraṣaram viti camayaṅkaḷ viṣayattilum (ēṉ camayaṅkaḷukku patiliyākac cila nāṭukaḷil irupatām nūṟ ṟāṇṭil kōlōcciya, iṉṟum kōlōccivarum icaṅkaḷukkum) poruntum eṉṟu aṟiyalām. “tamtam kaṭavuḷē kaṭavuḷ, ēṉiyōr kaṭavuḷkaḷ picācukaḷ.” tam kaṭavuḷi vaḻipaṭuvōr maṭṭumē nirantara mōṭcam celvar; ēṉiyōr mīḷā nirayattil erivar” eṉṟa naccuk koḷki kaḷuṭaṉ, vaṉmuṟiveṟiyuṭaṉ ceyalpaṭum camayaṅkaḷ attakiya kōṭpāṭukaḷ aṟṟa camayaṅkaḷi aḻittu oḻittu vantuḷḷati ulaka varalāṟu kāṭṭukiṟatu.

6. pallava aracaṉ makēntiravarmaṉ I ai civattiliruntu camaṇa camayattukku matam māṟṟiyavar “maruḷnīkkiyār” eṉṟa peyaril camaṇarāka iruntu piṉṉar tirunāvukkaracar eṉṟa peyaril civarāka māṟiya nāyaṉār. nāyaṉmār 63 pēruḷ talimiyāṉa mūvar mutalikaḷ eḻutiya tirumuṟip pāṭalkaḷ piṉvaruṉavaṟṟi cōḻa maṉṉaṉ irācarācaṉ I kālattil ki.pi. 10ām nūṟṟāṇṭil nampiyāṇṭār nampi tokuttār:-

							patikaṅkaḷ 		pāṭalkaḷ
tiruñāṉacampantar 			384 			4158
tirunāvukkarar (appar) 		307 			3066
nampiārūrar (cuntarar) 		100 			1026
							791 			8250

ivaṟṟit ‘tēvāram’ eṉa vaḻaṅkum marapu ki.pi. 16ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṉṉartāṉ uruvākiyatu. tamiḻilakkiya varalāṟu (1929) nūlil em.es. pūrṇaliṅkam ‘ñāṉa campantar pāṭalkaḷ (piṟa camayaṅkaḷukku etirāṉa) kaṭumiyāṉa vacavukaḷik koṇṭavi; tamatu cātiyiṉar mēmpāṭu, nalam ākiyaviyē avaṟṟiṉ kuṟikkōḷ. appar pāṭalkaḷō paṇpaṭṭa meyyiyaluṇarvum māntanēyamum koṇṭavi; maṉitarkaḷukkuḷ cāti mutaliya pētam pārāṭṭātavi’ eṉṟu kūṟiyuḷḷār. tamiḻiluḷḷa pakti nūlkaḷil (ēṉ ulaka moḻikaḷ aṉittilum uḷḷavaṟṟilēyē) mikac ciṟantatu ki.pi. 9ām nūṟṟāṇṭil māṇikkavācakar eḻutiya tiruvācakam ākum. ataṉi 1900il āṅkila ākkam ceyta jārj aklō pōp “āṅkilattil Pilgrim’s progress nūlukku oppāṉatu tiruvācakam; paktiyum teyvīka uṇarvum koṇṭa ñāṉiyiṉ aṉupavattil malarntatu” eṉṟu pārāṭṭiyuḷḷār. āṉmīkattiyum paktiyiyum “tattuvañāṉikaḷ kuḻāmiṭa miruntu” potumakkaḷiṭam koṇṭu ceṉṟatu tiruvācakam. upaniṣattukaḷ ‘vāḻum vaḻi a way of life ’ eṉpar irātā kiruṣṇaṉ. tiruvācaka āṅkila moḻi peyarppāḷaruḷ oruvarāṉa ji. vaṉmīkanātaṉ “mānta vāḻvē tiruvācakamtāṉ” Thiruvasagam is the life itself” eṉpār. tamiḻ “aṉpu” camskiruta priyah eṉpativiṭa nayamāṉa col. pakavat kīti 12.14-20: “sa me priyah” kku “he is dear to me, who” eṉpatutāṉ cariyāṉa āṅkila ākkam. āyiṉum Zaehner mutaliyavarkaḷ ‘I love the man who...” eṉa moḻipeyarttuḷḷār. māntaṉukkum kaṭavuḷukkum iṭiyiluḷḷa uṟavik kuṟikkum “aṉpu” pōṉṟa arumiyāṉa col kītiyil illi eṉpar ē.kē. rāmāṉucaṉ (Hymns for the drowning 1981: nammāḻvār pāṭalkaḷil 85ṉ nayamāṉa āṅkilap peyarppu) Nowhere does anything corresponding to the Tamil anpu (“love”) appear in the Sanskrit Gita as a possible relation between man and God.

7. pakti iyakkam paṟṟiya cempakalaṭcumiyiṉ 1996 kaṭṭurik karuttukkaḷ kuṟippiṭattakkaṉa. ki.pi. 600kku muṉṉar tamiḻaka aracaravikaḷilum nakaraṅkaḷilum (e.kā. kāñci, maturi) camaṇa putta camayaṅkaḷiṉ ki ōṅki iruntatu. accamayaṅkaḷi etirttu civa viṇava camayaṅkaḷ tam kaṭavuḷar pāl paktiyip parappip pōrāṭiya cūḻniliyil eḻuntavi paktippāṭalkaḷ. puṟac camayaṅkaḷ iraṇṭum vīṭupēṟṟukkup piṟappum cātiyum taṭiyalla eṉṟavi; avaṟṟiṉ kōṭpāṭṭit taḻuvi cāti, varuṇa vēṟupāṭiṉṟi aṉivarum vīṭu pēṟu aṭiyalām eṉṟu pakti iyakkamum vaḻivakuttami appuṟaccamayaṅkaḷi vīḻtta utaviyirukkalām. (The hymnists were propagating bhakti in a situation of conflict and rivalry with non-orthodex sects like the Jains and Buddhists whose influence and dominance in royal and urban centres had been established in the pre 7th centruy period. xxxxx Presumbaly, bhakti, by throwing open the path of salvation to all irrespective of caste and social hierarchy, imbibed the ideals of the non-orthodox creeds, namely birth and caste as no obstacles to salvation, and thereby succeeded in rooting out ‘herctical’ sects.) 

8. itē kālakaṭṭattil malarntaviyē viṇava pakti ilakkiyamum ākum. āḻvārkaḷ 12 pēril perumpālōr ikkālattil vāḻntavarkaḷ - iṉṟiya tūttukkuṭi māvaṭṭam kurukūril 8ām nūṟṟāṇṭil piṟanta māṟaṉ eṉṉum nammāḻvār (880-930) uṭpaṭa. nālāyira tivya pirapantattil uḷḷa 4000 pāṭalkaḷil nammāḻvār pāṭiya tiruvāymoḻi 1120 pāṭalkaḷum, tirumaṅkiyāḻvār pāṭiya 1110 pāṭalkaḷum aṭaṅkum. rāmāṉucaṉ tamatu 1981 nūlil pakti ilakkiyattiṉ piṉpulamāka piṉvarum kūṟukaḷ amintaṉa eṉkiṟār

vēta, upaniṣatak karuttakaḷ
ii. putta, camaṇak karuttukaḷ (caraṇam - pirapatti; caṅkam – 
aṭiyār kuḻām; kuru - cīṭar nerukkam)
purāṇak kaṟpaṉikaḷ
tamiḻ akapporuḷ, puṟapporuḷ marapukaḷ
pakavatkīti, paripāṭal, tirumurukāṟṟuppaṭi 
mutaliyavaṟṟiṉ karuttukaḷ
nāṭṭār (folk) camayam / meṭṭukaḷ / kaṟpaṉikaḷ /
karuttukaḷ.

āḻvārkaḷ pāṭalkaḷi aṭittaḷamākak koṇṭu 10ām nūṟṟāṇṭu nātamuṉi, avarpēraṉ yamuṉāccāriyār (10-11 nūṟṟāṇṭu) irāmāṉujācāriyār (1050-1137) ākiyōr uruvākkiyatu viciṣṭātvitam (qualified monism) eṉṉum viṇavak koḷki. caṅkarar uṭiya varaṭṭu atvitam strict monism koḷkiyiviṭa atu nayamāṉatu. kaṭavuḷākiya nārāyaṇaṉiṭam pakti celuttuvatu upaniṣattukkaḷiṉ vētāntattukku etirāṉatu aṉṟu eṉpati irāmāṉujar niṟuviṉār. viṇava īṭukaḷ (āḻvār pācuraṅkaḷukku virivurikaḷ) maṇippiravāḷa naṭiyil iruppiṉum ciṟanta meyyiyal nūlkaḷākum.

9. caṅka ilakkiya iṟutik kālattilēyē paktip pāṭalkaḷum tōṉṟiviṭṭaṉa (paripāṭal; tirumurukāṟṟup paṭi ) aṭuttu kaḷappirar kālattil 4-5 nūṟṟāṇṭil vāḻnta kārikkālammiyārum poykiyār, pēyār, pūtattār ākiya mutaṉ mūṉṟu āḻvārkaḷum paktippāṭalkaḷip pāṭiṉar. ki.pi. 6-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil civa-viṇava pakti ilakkiyam perum aḷavil vaḷarntatu. pakti tōṉṟiyatu tamiḻakattil (“tirāviṭattil”) eṉa patmapurāṇamum pākavata makātmiyamum kuṟippiṭukiṉṟaṉa. pākavatam kūṟuvatu

utpannā tirāviṭē sāham vrttim karnāṭakē katā kvaciṉ kvaciṉ makārāṣṭrē kūrjarē jirṇatām katā
		
	(nāṉ piṟantatu tirāviṭattil; vaḷarntatu karnāṭakattil; mahārāṣṭirattil eppaṭiyō nilittēṉ; kūrjarattil eṉ valimi kuṟintatu)

pakti iyakkam ōraḷavukku maṉitam cārntatākavum, kīḻc cātiyiṉaruṭpaṭa aṉivariṭamum potumiyiyum oṟṟumiyiyum vaḷarppatākavum amintatu. ki.pi. 12ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṟaku avviyakkam vaṭanāṭṭukkum paraviya poḻutu mukamatiyap parappāḷarkaḷiṉ tākkutaliliruntu intu matattip pātukāttiṭa ōraḷavukku utaviyatu.

10. ulakac cevvilakkiyaṅkaḷil tamiḻc cevvilakkiyaṅ kaḷāṉa caṅka kāla ilakkiyaṅkaḷ taṉittaṉmiyāṉa ciṟappu vāyntavi eṉpati mēlē kaṇṭōm. ulaka moḻikaḷiṉ pakti ilakkiyaṅkaḷ aṉittilum ki.pi. 400-900 cārnta tamiḻ pakti ilakkiyaṅkaḷ taṉic ciṟappu vāyntavi eṉpatiyum nallaṟiñar ēṟṟuḷḷaṉar.

11. tamiḻakattip poṟuttavari caṅka kāla camutāya - ilakkiyap pārviyiliruntu ki.pi. 6ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup pintiya pakti ilakkiya kāla camutāya ilakkiyap pārvi māṟupaṭṭatāka vaḷarntatu eṉpati piṉvarumāṟu cuvelapil kūṟukiṟār. (Tamil Traditions on Subrahmanya - Murugan; 1991) 

“tamiḻakattil toṭakka kālap pakti iyakkam meyyāṉa ārvamum uyirum uḷḷatāka iruntatu; nāyaṉmārkaḷ āḻvārkaḷuḷ ciṟanta cilar uṇarvupūrvamāṉa uṉṉata paktip pāṭalkaḷi āyirak kaṇakkil pāṭiṉar. āyiṉum nāḷaṭivil atu paṭippaṭiyāka uḷḷīṭiṉṟit taram tāḻa ārampittatu. uṇarvaṟṟa coṟkaṭṭukaḷ, kūṟiyatu kūṟal; carakkiṉmi, nakalkaḷ pōla aminta ērāḷamāṉa pāṭṭukaḷ; kuyuktiyāṉa, kiṟukkut taṉamāṉa kaṟpaṉikaḷ; aruvarukkattakka vakiyil mikiyāṉa uṇarcci veḷippāṭukaḷ; uṇmikkut toṭarpillāta kāṭci varuṇaṉikaḷ; iviyellām nāḷaṭivil paktip pāṭalkaḷil atikarittuk koṇṭē vantu kaṭiciyil nam kālattil ‘pakti’ eṉpatu iṉṉum taram tāḻntatāka ākiviṭṭatu. kaviti, putiṉam, ciṟukati, tirip paṭam, pāṭalkaḷ, nāṭakaṅkaḷ, ciṟpakkali mutaliya aṉittut tuṟikaḷilum. pāmarattaṉamāṉa kaḻicaṭi ciṉimākkaḷē (kāṭṭuttaṉamāṉa oliperukkik kūccalkaḷ; nayamaṟṟa miṉ viḷakku alaṅkāraṅkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟuṭaṉ) paktikku uruvam taruvaṉavāka amintuviṭṭaṉa”

“maṉa niliyip poṟutta variyilum cari, aṟivu pūrvamākavum cari paktiyiṉ ittakiya pariṇāma vaḷarcciyum ataṉ viḷivukaḷum tamiḻar paṇpāṭṭukkuk kēṭāka amintu viṭṭaṉa eṉṟu karutāmalirukka iyalavilli. piṟa pakutikaḷ, nāṭukaḷiṉ paṇpāṭukaḷil pakti eṉpatu pala kūṟukaḷil oṉṟāka maṭṭumē amintatu. māṟākat tamiḻakattilō ki.pi. 700/900liruntu 1900 vari pakti, pakti, paktiyit tavira vēṟu etuvum kuṟippiṭattakkatāka illi. ilakkiyattil maṭṭum (potuvāka pulavarulakam ēṟṟa ilakkiyaṅkaḷ variciyil cērkkappaṭāmal otukki vittaviyāṉa) nāṭṭup pāṭalkaḷ, kātal pāṭalkaḷ, paḷḷukaḷ, kuṟavañcikaḷ cila, ākiyavi paktiyallāta poruṇmi cārnta cila paṭippukaḷāka uruvāyiṉa.”

12. intiyā muḻuvatum camayattilum, meyyiyalilum, peruntākkam viḷivitta āticaṅkarācāriyār (788-820) aṉṟiya tamiḻakattiṉ mēlik kaṭaṟpakutiyil (iṉṟiya kēraḷattil) kālaṭi eṉṉum ūril piṟanta pirāmaṇar āvār; atvitak koḷkiyiṉar. avar karunāṭakattil micūrilum, kattiyavāril tuvārakiyilum, oricāvil pūriyilum imaya maliccāralil patrināttilum niṟuviya nāṉku maṭaṅkaḷ avarukkup piṉṉar akkoḷkiyip parappiṉa. vijayanakara āṭcik kālattil kumpakōṇattil niṟuvappaṭṭup piṉṉar kāñcipurattukku māṟṟappaṭṭa caṅkaramaṭam mutaṉ mutalil ciruṅkēri maṭattiṉ kiḷiyākat tōṉṟiyatē eṉpatu pala aṟiñarkaḷiṉ karuttu ākum. (ār. kiruṣṇacāmi aiyar (1959) srī kumpakōṇa maṭam - oru vimarcaṉam . jē.vi. rāja kōpāla carmā 1963 jakatkuru caṅkara maṭa vimarsa (intiyil); 1987: Kanchi Kamakoti math, a myth; ār. kiruṣṇacāmi aiyar & kē. ār. vēṅkaṭarāmaṉ 1965The truth about the Kumbhakonam mutt) 

13. cōḻa nāṭṭiṉ perumpakuti ki.pi. 7-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil pallavar āṭciyiṉ kīḻ iruntatu. teṟkil pāṇṭiya nāṭṭi oṭṭiyirunta cōḻa nāṭṭiṉ ciṟupakutiyō pāṇṭiyar āṭciyiṉ kīḻ iruntatu. eṉavē cōḻanāṭṭip paṟṟit taṉiyāka ivviyalil kuṟippiṭavilli.

14. caṅka kālattiṟku piṉṉar cēranāṭu citaṟuṇṭu palaciṟu aracarkaḷiṉ āṭciyiṉ kīḻ vantatu. acciṟṟaracukaḷ cila kālaṅkaḷil cutantaramākavum cila kālaṅkaḷil pāṇṭiya, cōḻa maṉṉarkaḷip pēraracarāka ēṟṟum ceyalpaṭṭuvantaṉa. paṇṭiya cēra nāṭṭiṉ kuṟippiṭattakka pakutiyi āṇṭa kaṭicic cēra maṉṉar cēramāṉ perumāṉ (nāyaṉār). avar cuntarar naṇpaṉ ki.pi. 825il avar taṉ nāṭṭittaṉ uṟaviṉarkaḷiṭamum ciṟṟaracarkaḷiṭamum pirittuk koṭuttu viṭṭuc cuntararuṭaṉ kilācattiṟkup pōyviṭṭatākak kati uḷḷatu. innikaḻcciyiliruntu (ki.pi. 825) toṭaṅkiyatē kollam āṇṭu eṉak karutukiṉṟaṉar.

15. ikkāla kaṭṭattil tamiḻnāṭṭiṉ vaṭapakutiyi āṇṭuvanta pallavarkaḷiṉ tōṟṟattip paṟṟiya vēṟupaṭṭa karuttukaḷi muṉ iyalil kaṇṭōm. nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri tarum pallavar koṭivaḻi varumāṟu:

859-99 				885-903 			870-912

16. mutal makēntiravarmaṉ kālam mutal paramēcuvaraṉ II kālamvari ēṟattāḻa nūṟu āṇṭukaḷ pallavarkaḷum (iṉṟiya makārāṭṭira, karṉāṭakap pakutiyiṉi āṇṭa) vātāpic cāḷukyarum oruvarōṭu oruvar pōriṭṭu vantaṉar. cāḷukyar avvappoḻutu tamiḻakattukkuk koḷḷi yaṭittuc celvataṟkākap paṭiyeṭuppatu vaḻakkam. makēntiraṉ I kālattil pulikēci II kāñcivarip paṭiyeṭuttāṉ; makēntiraṉ pullalūriṉ avaṉiveṉṟu tirumpip pōkac ceytāṉ. naracimmaṉ I vātāpikkup paṭi koṇṭu ceṉṟu appulikēciyi 642-3il tōṟkaṭittāṉ. ivaṉ kālattil 639-40il kāñcikkum vantāṉ cīṉappayaṇi hiyūṉ cāṅ. pallavaruḷ iḷiya kālavaḻiyic cārnta nantivarmaṉ II 731il ariyaṇi ēṟiṉāṉ. kāñciyil avaṉ kaṭṭiya vikuṇṭap perumāḷ kōyil kalveṭṭu avaṉ ariyaṇi
ēṟak kāñcikku varumpoḻutu kāṭumalikaḷit tāṇṭiyum kaṭalittāṇṭiyum vantatākak kūṟukiṟatu. avaṉ muṉṉō rākiya pallavak kāl vaḻiyiṉar kampūcciyā (iṉṟiya kampōṭiyā) vil āṇṭu vantavar eṉṟu uṉṉippar aṟiñar ti.nā. cuppiramaṇiyam. tāylāntil takuvā pā Takua Pa  eṉṉum iṭattil uḷḷa ki.pi. 9ām nūṟṟāṇṭu cārnta kalveṭṭu tamiḻa vāṇikac cāttiṉar aṅkirunta viṣṇu kōyilukkāka avaṉi nāraṇap pērēri veṭṭu vittatākak kuṟippiṭukiṟatu. nanti varmaṉ III paṭṭap peyar kaḷuḷ oṉṟu avaṉi nārāṇaṉ; eṉavē akkalveṭṭu avaṉik kuṟittatu eṉalām. teḷḷāṟṟup pōril pāṇṭiyaṉi avaṉ veṉṟatāl pāṇṭiya araciṉ atikāram pāṇṭiya nāṭṭiṟkuḷ aṭaṅkiyatu. vijayālaya cōḻaṉ makaṉ ātittaṉ I kipi 890il aparājitaṉi veṉṟatuṭaṉ tamiḻakattil pallavar āṭci muṭivuṟṟatu eṉalām.

17. ikkālap pallavar - pāṇṭiyar varalāṟṟip paṟṟiya virivāṉa ceytikaḷi tamiḻaka araciṉ “varalāṟṟuk kuḻu’ 1990il veḷiyiṭṭaḷḷa “pallavar – pāṇṭiyarkaḷ kālam: kipi 500-900) nūliṉ irumaṭalaṅkaḷil kāṇalām. mutal maṭalam 434 pakkam koṇṭatu (pallavar paṟṟi ār kōpālaṉum pāṇṭiyar paṟṟi kē.vi. irāmaṉum eḻuti yuḷḷaṉar.) iraṇṭām maṭalam 538 pakkaṅkaḷ koṇṭatu; camutāyam, poruḷiyal, camayam ilakkiyam, kalikaḷ paṟṟip palar eḻutiyavaṟṟik koṇṭatu. ici paṟṟi vī.pa.ka. cuntaram pakkaṅkaḷ 481-537il eḻutiyuḷḷār.

18. tamiḻakak kōyiṟkaṭṭaṭakkali ciṟpakkali vaḷarcciyil pallava maṉṉar paṅkaḷippu mukkiyamāṉatākum. makēntiravarmaṉ amitta kukikkōyilkaḷ (tiruccirāp paḷḷi, makēntiravāṭi, māmaṇṭūr, viḻuppuram arukē maṇṭakappaṭṭu) ākiyavi kuṟippiṭattakkaviyākum. māmallapurattiluḷḷa ulakap pukaḻpeṟṟa kuṭivarik kōyilkaḷ naracimmaṉ I (māmallaṉ) kālattiliruntu amikkappaṭṭaviyākum. kaṟṟaḷikaḷi eḻuppiyatilum pallavar muṉṉōṭikaḷ; ivaṟṟuḷ mikac ciṟantavi māmallapuram kaṭaṟkarik kōyil, kāñci kilācanātar kōyil (iraṇṭum rācacimmaṉ kaṭṭiyavi) kāñci vikuṇṭap perumāḷ kōyil (nantivarmaṉ II kaṭṭiyatu) ākiyaviyām. teṉ intiya vāṇikattil akkālattil taliciṟantu viḷaṅkiya tuṟimukaṅkaḷil māmallapuramum oṉṟu eṉpar nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri. intōṉēciyā (jāvātīvu) intō cīṉā (kampōṭiyā) nāṭukaḷil intiya kōyiṟ kaṭṭaṭa, ciṟpak kaliyi piṉpaṟṟi eḻunta māperum kōyilkaḷāṉa pōrōputūr; aṅkōr vāṭ pōṉṟavi eḻuvataṟkāṉa paṇpāṭṭut tākkamum, toḻil nuṭpamum iṅkiruntutāṉ ceṉṟirukka vēṇṭum eṉpār avar. ayal nāṭukaḷil kaṭṭiya akkōyilkaḷ tāyt tamiḻnāṭṭuk kōyilkaḷiyum viñciya ciṟappuṭiyaviyāka amintaṉa. māmallapurattil “pakīrataṉ tavam” eṉṟu aḻikkappaṭum puṭippuc ciṟpatti piṉvarumāṟu kiros (Grousset)  pārāṭṭiyuḷḷār.

“kallilē varinta piramāṇṭamāṉa cuti ōviyamtāṉ ippuṭic ciṟpam. eṭuttukkoṇṭa viṣayattukkēṟpa ērāḷamāṉa uruvaṅkaḷ nayamākac cittarikkappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. ulaka nalaṉukkāka iṟaṅkivarukiṟa  (kaṅki) nīric cūḻaniṟkum uyirkaḷ aṉittum kāṭṭum ārvam teḷivākat terikiṟatu; iyaṟkiyi āḻnta kavarcciyuṭaṉ kāṭṭukiṟatu icciṟpam.”

pakīrataṉ tavam paṟṟiya kati intu, camaṇa camayaṅkaḷ iraṇṭukkum potuvāṉatu. camaṇaruṭiya iraṇṭām tīrttaṅkarar ajitanātar. ajitanāta purāṇattil uḷḷa cakara - cākarar katiyiyē māmallapuram ciṟpam kāṭṭukiṟatu eṉpār ma.cī. vēṅkaṭacāmi. pallavar kālattil puṭippuc ciṟpakkali aṭinta mika uṉṉatamāṉa niliyi itu kāṭṭukiṟatu.
